u^{k}:=Fx^{k}
leads to
e^{k}:=u^{k}-Fx^{k}=0
, making our analysis simpler.
Moreover, it allows us to expand the range of
L\rho
to
2L\rho<1
.
Hence, we state it separately in this subsection.
This variant is similar to the ones in
[
1
,
88
]
, but the update of
\bar{x}^{k}
uses
w^{k}
instead of
w^{k+1}
as in
[
1
,
88
]
.

Theorem 5.3

For (
NI
), suppose that
\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)\neq\emptyset
,
F
is
L
-Lipschitz continuous, and
\Phi
is
\rho
-co-hypomonotone such that
2L\rho<1
.
Let
\{(x^{k},y^{k},\bar{x}^{k})\}
be generated by (
GFEG+
) using
u^{k}:=Fx^{k}
and

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\tau_{k}:=\frac{1}{t_{k}}=\frac{1}{\mu(k+r)},\quad\hat{\eta}_{k}:=\eta(1-\tau_{k}),\ \ \text{and}\ \ \beta_{k}:=-\gamma\tau_{k}+2\rho(1-\tau_{k}),\end{array}

(56)

where
r\geq\frac{1}{\mu}
and
\mu\in(0,1)
are given, and
\eta
and
\gamma
are chosen such that

\begin{array}[]{ll}2\rho<\eta\leq\frac{1}{L}\quad\textrm{and}\quad 0<\gamma<(1-\mu)(\eta-2\rho).\end{array}

(57)

Then, we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\|Fx^{k}+\xi^{k}\|^{2}\leq\dfrac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}2}(1-\mu)\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}}{[(1-\mu)(\eta-2\rho)-\gamma]\mu^{2}(k+r-1)^{2}},\\
&{\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[2\mu(k+r)-1-\mu]}\|Fx^{k}+\xi^{k}\|^{2}\leq\dfrac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}2}\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(1-\mu)(\eta-2\rho)-\gamma}},\end{array}

(58)

where
\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}:=\frac{\mu(r-1)[\mu(r-1)(\eta-2\rho)+2\rho]}{2}\|Fx^{0}+\xi^{0}\|^{2}+\frac{1-\mu}{2\gamma}\|x^{0}-x^{\star}\|^{2}
.

Similar to Theorem
5.2
, we can prove the summable results in (
54
), the
o\left(1/k^{2}\right)
convergence rates in (
55
), and the convergence of the iterate sequences
\{x^{k}\}
,
\{y^{k}\}
, and
\{\bar{x}^{k}\}
for this special case.
However, we omit the details since it repeats the proof of Theorem
5.2
using Theorem
5.3
.

6
Nesterov’s Accelerated Extragradient Methods for Inclusions (
NI
)

Sections
3
,
4
, and
5
have studied Halpern’s accelerated (or anchored) methods.
Now, we will focus on Nesterov’s accelerated algorithms in the next two sections.
In this section, we unify and generalize Nesterov’s accelerated extragradient method from
[
77
]
to a broader class of algorithms for solving (
NI
).

6.1
A Class of Nesterov’s Accelerated Extragradient Algorithms

\mathrm{(a)}

The proposed method.

First, for given
x^{k}\in\mathrm{dom}(\Phi)
,
u^{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, and
\xi^{k}\in Tx^{k}
, we recall the following quantities from (
20
):

w^{k}:=Fx^{k}+\xi^{k},\quad\hat{w}^{k}:=Fy^{k-1}+\xi^{k},\quad\text{and}\quad z^{k}:=u^{k}+\xi^{k}.

(59)

Next, starting from
x^{0}\in\mathrm{dom}(\Phi)
, let
u^{0}:=Fx^{0}
and
y^{-1}=\hat{x}^{-1}=\hat{x}^{0}:=x^{0}
.
At each iteration
k\geq 0
, we update the sequence
\{(x^{k},\hat{x}^{k},y^{k})\}
as

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}x^{k+1}&:=&y^{k}-\eta(\hat{w}^{k+1}-\gamma_{k}z^{k}),\\
\hat{x}^{k+1}&:=&x^{k+1}-\lambda z^{k+1},\\
y^{k+1}&:=&\hat{x}^{k+1}+\theta_{k}(\hat{x}^{k+1}-\hat{x}^{k})+\nu_{k}(y^{k}-\hat{x}^{k+1}),\end{array}\right.

(GAEG)

where
\eta>0
,
\gamma_{k}\in[0,1]
,
\lambda>0
,
\theta_{k}\in(0,1)
, and
\nu_{k}\geq 0
are given parameters (determined later), and
u^{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
is a search direction satisfying

\|Fx^{k}-u^{k}\|^{2}\leq\kappa\|Fx^{k}-Fy^{k-1}\|^{2}+\hat{\kappa}\|w^{k}-w^{k-1}\|^{2},

(60)

for given constants
\kappa\geq 0
and
\hat{\kappa}\geq 0
, and
w^{-1}:=w^{0}
.
In particular, if
T=0
, then
w^{k}-w^{k-1}=Fx^{k}-Fx^{k-1}
, and thus
u^{k}
depends on
Fx^{k}
,
Fy^{k-1}
, and
Fx^{k-1}
.
Note that we allow
\kappa\geq 0
to be arbitrary, but
\hat{\kappa}
will be chosen in a certain range (
cf.
Theorem
6.1
).

\mathrm{(b)}

Three special instances.

As before, our method (
GAEG
) possibly covers a wide class of schemes by instantiating different directions
u^{k}
, which satisfy (
60
).
We consider at least the following three special choices of
u^{k}
.

\mathrm{(i)}

Variant 1.

If we choose
u^{k}:=Fx^{k}
, then
z^{k}=w^{k}
and (
60
) holds with
\kappa=\hat{\kappa}=0
.
In this case, (
GAEG
) reduces to

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}x^{k+1}&:=&y^{k}-\eta(\hat{w}^{k+1}-\gamma_{k}w^{k}),\\
\hat{x}^{k+1}&:=&x^{k+1}-\lambda w^{k+1},\\
y^{k+1}&:=&\hat{x}^{k+1}+\theta_{k}(\hat{x}^{k+1}-\hat{x}^{k})+\nu_{k}(y^{k}-\hat{x}^{k+1}).\end{array}\right.

(61)

Clearly, (
61
) is exactly the accelerated extragradient method in
[
77
]
.

\mathrm{(ii)}

Variant 2.

If we choose
u^{k}:=Fy^{k-1}
, then
z^{k}=\hat{w}^{k}
and (
60
) holds with
\kappa=1
and
\hat{\kappa}=0
.
Then, (
GAEG
) reduces to

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}x^{k+1}&:=&y^{k}-\eta(\hat{w}^{k+1}-\gamma_{k}\hat{w}^{k}),\\
\hat{x}^{k+1}&:=&x^{k+1}-\lambda\hat{w}^{k+1},\\
y^{k+1}&:=&\hat{x}^{k+1}+\theta_{k}(\hat{x}^{k+1}-\hat{x}^{k})+\nu_{k}(y^{k}-\hat{x}^{k+1}).\end{array}\right.

(62)

This variant seems to be new and it is different from the second algorithm in
[
77
]
.
This method is also different from the one in
[
71
]
when specified to solve the monotone (
VIP
).
However, we only obtain
\mathcal{O}\left(1/k\right)
convergence rate, while
[
71
]
can achieve both
\mathcal{O}\left(1/k\right)
and
o\left(1/k\right)
convergence rates.

\mathrm{(iii)}

Variant 3: Generalization.

Let
v^{k-1}:=w^{k-1}-\xi^{k}
.
We can construct

\begin{array}[]{lcl}u^{k}:=\alpha Fx^{k}+\hat{\alpha}Fy^{k-1}+(1-\alpha-\hat{\alpha})v^{k-1}\end{array}

as an affine combination of
Fx^{k}
,
Fy^{k-1}
and
v^{k-1}
for given constants
\alpha,\hat{\alpha}\in\mathbb{R}
.
Then, by Young’s inequality, for any
m>0
, one can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|Fx^{k}-u^{k}\|^{2}&=&\|\hat{\alpha}(Fx^{k}-Fy^{k-1})+(1-\alpha-\hat{\alpha})(Fx^{k}-v^{k-1})\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\kappa\|Fx^{k}-Fy^{k-1}\|^{2}+\hat{\kappa}\|w^{k}-w^{k-1}\|^{2},\end{array}

where
\kappa:=(1+m)\hat{\alpha}^{2}
and
\hat{\kappa}:=(1\!+\!m^{-1})(1\!-\!\alpha\!-\!\hat{\alpha})^{2}
.
Thus
u^{k}
satisfies (
60
).

\mathrm{(c)}

The implementation of (
GAEG
).

To implement (
GAEG
), we assume that the resolvent
J_{\eta T}
of
\eta T
is
well-defined
(see Section
3
) for any
\eta>0
.
Generally, we do not require
J_{\eta T}
to be single-valued, but to avoid complicating our presentation, we again assume that
J_{\eta T}
is single-valued.

Using the resolvent
J_{\eta T}
, we can rewrite (
GAEG
) equivalently to

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}x^{k+1}&:=&J_{\eta T}\big(y^{k}-\eta(Fy^{k}-\gamma_{k}(u^{k}+\xi^{k}))\big),\\
\xi^{k+1}&:=&\frac{1}{\eta}(y^{k}-x^{k+1})-Fy^{k}+\gamma_{k}(u^{k}+\xi^{k}),\\
\hat{x}^{k+1}&:=&x^{k+1}-\lambda(u^{k+1}+\xi^{k+1}),\\
y^{k+1}&:=&\hat{x}^{k+1}+\theta_{k}(\hat{x}^{k+1}-\hat{x}^{k})+\nu_{k}(y^{k}-\hat{x}^{k+1}).\end{array}\right.

(63)

This scheme requires one evaluation
Fy^{k}
of
F
, one evaluation
J_{\eta T}
of
\eta T
, and one construction of
u^{k}
.
Under the condition (
60
), constructing
u^{k}
may require at most one more evaluation of
F
.
In particular, if
u^{k}:=Fx^{k}
, then we need one more evaluation
Fx^{k}
of
F
.
If
u^{k}:=Fy^{k-1}
, then we can reuse the value
Fy^{k-1}
evaluated from the previous iteration
k-1
.

6.2
Key Estimates for Convergence Analysis

\mathrm{(a)}

Key lemma.

To analyze (
GAEG
), we first state the following lemma.

Lemma 7

For (
NI
), suppose that
\Phi
is
\rho
-co-hypomonotone,
F
is
L
-Lipschitz continuous, and
\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)\neq\emptyset
.
Given
\lambda>0
,
\hat{c}>0
,
\beta>0
,
\mu\geq 0
, and
t_{0}>0
, let
\{(x^{k},\hat{x}^{k},y^{k})\}
be generated by (
GAEG
) using the following parameters:

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\eta:=(1+2\hat{c})\lambda+2\rho+2\beta+2\mu,\quad\quad t_{k+1}:=t_{k}+1,\\
&\gamma_{k}:=\frac{t_{k}-1}{t_{k}},\quad\theta_{k}:=\frac{t_{k}-1}{t_{k+1}},\quad\text{ and }\quad\nu_{k}:=\frac{t_{k}}{t_{k+1}}.\end{array}

(64)

For any
x^{\star}\in\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)
, we consider the following function:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{P}_{k}&:=&\frac{a_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+b_{k}\langle w^{k},\hat{x}^{k}-y^{k}\rangle+\|\hat{x}^{k}-x^{\star}+t_{k}(y^{k}-\hat{x}^{k})\|^{2},\end{array}

(65)

where
a_{k}:=\frac{b_{k}}{t_{k}}\big[\lambda t_{k}+(1+2\hat{c})\lambda+2\rho+2\mu\big]
and
b_{k+1}:=\frac{b_{k}}{\theta_{k}}
for
b_{k}>0
.

Then, for any
c>0
and
M_{c}:=(1+c)L^{2}
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{P}_{k}-\mathcal{P}_{k+1}&\geq&\mu b_{k}\|w^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{b_{k}(1-M_{c}\eta^{2})}{2M_{c}\eta\gamma_{k}}\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{b_{k}}{2}\big(\frac{\lambda}{\hat{c}}+\frac{\eta^{2}\gamma_{k}}{\beta}+\frac{\eta\gamma_{k}}{c}\big)\|u^{k}-Fx^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{b_{k}}{2}\big(\frac{\lambda}{\hat{c}}+\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\beta\gamma_{k}}\big)\|u^{k+1}-Fx^{k+1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

(66)

\mathrm{(b)}

Lyapunov function and descent property.

Next, for given
\mathcal{P}_{k}
defined in (
GAEG
) we consider the following Lyapunov function:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{k}&:=&\mathcal{P}_{k}+\frac{c_{k}}{2}\|u^{k}-Fx^{k}\|^{2}\\
&=&\frac{a_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+b_{k}\langle w^{k},\hat{x}^{k}-y^{k}\rangle+\|\hat{x}^{k}-x^{\star}+t_{k}(y^{k}-\hat{x}^{k})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{c_{k}}{2}\|u^{k}-Fx^{k}\|^{2},\end{array}

(67)

where
a_{k}:=\frac{b_{k}}{t_{k}}\left[\lambda t_{k}+(1+2\hat{c})\lambda+2\rho+2\mu\right]
given in Lemma
7
,
b_{k+1}:=\frac{b_{k}}{\theta_{k}}
for
b_{k}>0
, and
c_{k}:=b_{k}\big(\frac{\lambda}{\hat{c}}+\frac{\eta^{2}\gamma_{k}}{\beta}+\frac{\eta\gamma_{k}}{c}\big)
.

Then, we can prove the following descent property of
\mathcal{L}_{k}
.

Lemma 8

Under the same settings as in Lemma
7
, if
u^{k}
satisfies (
60
) and
t_{0}>1
, then
\mathcal{L}_{k}
defined by (
67
) satisfies

\hskip-4.30554pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{k}-\mathcal{L}_{k+1}&\geq&\frac{b_{k}t_{k}}{2t_{k-1}}\big[\frac{1}{M_{c}\eta}-\eta-\kappa\big(\frac{2\lambda}{\hat{c}}+\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\beta}+\frac{\eta^{2}}{\beta}+\frac{\eta}{c}\big)\big]\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{b_{k}t_{k}}{2t_{k-1}}\big[\frac{2\mu(t_{0}-1)}{t_{0}}-\hat{\kappa}\big(\frac{2\lambda}{\hat{c}}+\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\beta}+\frac{\eta^{2}}{\beta}+\frac{\eta}{c}\big)\big]\|w^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

(68)

\mathrm{(c)}

Lower bound of
\mathcal{L}_{k}
.

Finally, we can lower bound
\mathcal{L}_{k}
as follows.

Lemma 9

Under the same settings as in Lemma
8
and
b_{0}:=\frac{3\lambda t_{0}(t_{0}-1)}{2}
,
\mathcal{L}_{k}
defined by (
67
) is lower bounded by

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{k}&\geq&\frac{b_{k}}{2t_{k}}\big[\frac{\lambda t_{k}}{4}+(2\hat{c}-\frac{1}{2})\lambda+2\mu\big]\|w^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{b_{k}}{2t_{k}}\big[\frac{\lambda}{\hat{c}}(t_{k}-4\hat{c})+(\frac{\eta}{\beta}+\frac{1}{c})\eta t_{k-1}\big]\|u^{k}-Fx^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

(69)

6.3
Convergence Guarantees of (
GAEG
) and Its Special Cases

\mathrm{(a)}

The convergence of (
GAEG
).

Given
\kappa\geq 0
,
\sigma:=\frac{5}{24}
, and
L
and
\rho
such that
4(1+\sigma)\sqrt{2(5\kappa+1)}L\rho<1
, we define

\hskip-12.91663pt\begin{array}[]{ll}\bar{\lambda}:=\frac{2\bar{c}}{\bar{b}+\sqrt{\bar{b}^{2}+4\bar{c}}},\ \ \text{where}\ \ \bar{b}:=\frac{(1+\sigma)(49\kappa+8)\rho}{29\kappa+4}\ \textrm{and}\ \bar{c}:=\frac{1-32(1+\sigma)^{2}(5\kappa+1)L^{2}\rho^{2}}{8L^{2}(29\kappa+4)}.\end{array}\hskip-12.91663pt

(70)

Now, we are ready to prove the convergence of (
GAEG
) as follows.

Theorem 6.1

For (
NI
), suppose that
\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)\neq\emptyset
,
F
is
L
-Lipschitz continuous,
\Phi
is
\rho
-co-hypomonotone, and
J_{\eta T}
is well-defined.
Let
\{(x^{k},\hat{x}^{k},y^{k})\}
be generated by (
GAEG
) using
u^{k}
satisfying (
60
) with
\kappa\geq 0
and
\hat{\kappa}\geq 0
.
Given
r>1
and
\bar{\lambda}
defined in (
70
), suppose that

\hskip-8.61108pt\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}&0\leq\hat{\kappa}\leq\frac{r-1}{58r},\ \quad L\rho<\frac{6}{29\sqrt{2(5\kappa+1)}},\ \quad 0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda},\ \quad\eta:=4\lambda+\frac{29}{6}\rho,\\
&t_{k}:=k+r,\qquad\gamma_{k}:=\frac{t_{k}-1}{t_{k}},\qquad\theta_{k}:=\frac{t_{k}-1}{t_{k+1}},\quad\textrm{ and }\quad\nu_{k}:=\frac{t_{k}}{t_{k+1}}.\end{array}\right.\hskip-8.61108pt

(71)

Then, for any
x^{\star}\in\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)
, the following bound holds:

\|Fx^{k}+\xi^{k}\|^{2}\leq\frac{\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}}{3\lambda^{2}(k+r-1)(k+r+2)},

(72)

where
\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}:=\lambda(r-1)\big[12(r+2)\lambda+29\rho\big]\|Fx^{0}+\xi^{0}\|^{2}+16\|x^{0}-x^{\star}\|^{2}
.

Remark 5

The momentum parameter
\theta_{k}
in (
71
) is exactly
\theta_{k}=\frac{k+r-1}{k+r+1}
, aligned with the one in standard Nesterov’s accelerated methods.
However, this choice does not allow us to prove faster convergence rates (i.e.
o\left(1/k\right)
rates) as well as the convergence of iterate sequences as recognized in
[
5
,
21
]
.

\mathrm{(b)}

Special instances.

Finally, we consider two special cases of (
GAEG
) corresponding to
u^{k}=Fx^{k}
and
u^{k}=Fy^{k-1}
.
The proof of this result is similar to Theorem
6.1
, and we provide its details in the appendix.

Corollary 4

For (
NI
), suppose that
\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)\neq\emptyset
,
F
is
L
-Lipschitz continuous,
\Phi
is
\rho
-co-hypomonotone, and
J_{\eta T}
is well-defined.
Let
\{(x^{k},\hat{x}^{k},y^{k})\}
be generated by (
GAEG
).

\mathrm{(i)}

If we choose
u^{k}:=Fx^{k}
, then we require
2L\rho<1
, and choose
0<\lambda\leq\frac{1}{L}-2\rho
,
\eta:=\lambda+2\rho
, and other parameters as in (
71
) with
r>2
.
Then

\|Fx^{k}+\xi^{k}\|^{2}\leq\frac{\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}(k+r-2)(k+r-1)},

(73)

where
\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}:=4\lambda(r-1)[(r+1)\lambda+2\rho]\|Fx^{0}+\xi^{0}\|^{2}+\frac{16}{3}\|x^{0}-x^{\star}\|^{2}
.

\mathrm{(ii)}

If we choose
u^{k}:=Fy^{k-1}
, then we require
8\sqrt{3}L\rho<1
, and choose
0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}
,
\eta:=3\lambda+4\rho
, and other parameters as in (
71
),
where

\hskip-4.30554pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\bar{\lambda}:=\frac{2\bar{c}}{\bar{b}+\sqrt{\bar{b}^{2}+4\bar{c}}}\quad\text{with}\quad\bar{b}:=\frac{272\rho}{123}\geq 0\quad\textrm{and}\quad\bar{c}:=\frac{1-129L^{2}\rho^{2}}{164L^{2}}>0.\end{array}

In particular, if
\rho=0

(
i.e.
\Phi
is monotone
)
, then we choose
0<\lambda\leq\bar{\lambda}:=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{41}L}
and
\eta:=3\lambda
.
Then, the following bound holds:

\|Fx^{k}+\xi^{k}\|^{2}\leq\frac{\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}(k+r-1)(k+r)},

(74)

where
\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}:=2\lambda(r-1)[(2r+3)\lambda+4\rho]\|Fx^{0}+\xi^{0}\|^{2}+\frac{16}{3}\|x^{0}-x^{\star}\|^{2}
.

Remark 6

The range of
L\rho
in Theorem
6.1
and in Part (b) of Corollary
4
can potentially be improved by properly choosing parameters
c
,
\hat{c}
,
\beta
, and
\mu
in our proof.
Here, we have not tried to optimize these ranges of
L\rho
,
\lambda
, and
\eta
.

7
New Accelerated EG-Type Methods for (
NI
): Better Guarantees

In this section, we develop a novel class of generalized Nesterov’s accelerated extragradient methods to solve (
NI
), which can achieve faster convergence rates than the one in Section
6
.
In addition, we can prove the convergence of the iterate sequences, which is still open in Section
6
.
Note that this framework is different from the moving anchor FBFS method in Section
5
as it relies on Nesterov’s acceleration techniques.
A detailed comparison between two types of methods can be found in
[
82
]
.

7.1
A Class of Nesterov’s Accelerated Extragradient Algorithms

\mathrm{(a)}

The proposed method.

First, for given
x^{k}\in\mathrm{dom}(\Phi)
,
u^{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, and
\xi^{k}\in Tx^{k}
, we recall the following quantities from the previous sections:

w^{k}:=Fx^{k}+\xi^{k},\quad\hat{w}^{k}:=Fy^{k-1}+\xi^{k},\quad\text{and}\quad z^{k}:=u^{k}+\xi^{k}.

(75)

Next, starting from
x^{0}\in\mathrm{dom}(\Phi)
, we set
u^{0}:=Fx^{0}
and
y^{-1}=y^{0}:=x^{0}
, and at each iteration
k\geq 0
, we update the iterate sequence
\{(x^{k},y^{k})\}
as

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}x^{k+1}&:=&y^{k}-\eta d^{k},\\
y^{k+1}&:=&x^{k+1}+\theta_{k}(x^{k+1}-x^{k})-p^{k},\end{array}\right.

(GAEG
+
)

where
d^{k}
and
p^{k}
are respectively defined by

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}d^{k}&:=&\hat{w}^{k+1}-\gamma_{k}z^{k},\\
p^{k}&:=&\eta_{k}z^{k+1}-\lambda_{k}\hat{w}^{k+1}+\nu_{k}z^{k}.\end{array}\right.

(76)

The involved parameters in (
GAEG+
) and (
76
) are updated by

\begin{array}[]{ll}&t_{k+1}:=t_{k}+1,\qquad\theta_{k}:=\frac{t_{k}-r-\mu}{t_{k+1}},\qquad\gamma_{k}:=\frac{t_{k}-r+1}{t_{k}},\\
&\eta_{k}:=\frac{(\eta-\beta)t_{k}-\delta}{t_{k+1}},\qquad\lambda_{k}:=\frac{\eta t_{k}}{t_{k+1}},\quad\textrm{and}\quad\nu_{k}:=\frac{\beta t_{k}}{t_{k+1}},\end{array}

(77)

where
\eta>\beta\geq 0
,
r>0
, and
\mu>0
are determined later and
\delta:=(r-1)\beta+\frac{(r-2)(\eta-\beta)}{\mu+1}
.
Moreover, the direction
u^{k}
in (
75
) satisfies the following condition:

\|Fx^{k}-u^{k}\|^{2}\leq\kappa\|Fx^{k}-Fy^{k-1}\|^{2}+\hat{\kappa}\|d^{k-1}\|^{2},

(78)

for given constants
\kappa\geq 0
and
\hat{\kappa}\geq 0
, and
d^{-1}:=0
.
Here, we allow
\kappa
to be arbitrary, but we require
\hat{\kappa}
to be sufficiently small, determined later.

Remark 7

At first glance, one may question: why are the parameters updated as in (
77
)?
Let us explain.
The choice of
\theta_{k}
is suggested by Nesterov’s accelerated methods in convex optimization and monotone inclusions, which is often of the form
\theta_{k}=\frac{k-s}{k+2}
for
s\geq 0
.
The direction
d^{k}
can be written as
d^{k}=\big(1-\frac{1}{\gamma_{k}}\big)\hat{w}^{k+1}+\gamma_{k}(\hat{w}^{k+1}-z^{k})
, which can be viewed as a generalization of the forward-reflected-backward splitting step.
Here,
\gamma_{k}(\hat{w}^{k+1}-z^{k})
can be seen as an extrapolation or a correction term.
The parameters
\eta_{k}
,
\lambda_{k}
, and
\nu_{k}
in
p^{k}
satisfy
\eta_{k}-\lambda_{k}+\nu_{k}=\frac{\delta}{t_{k+1}}
as often seen in Nesterov’s accelerated methods for monotone inclusions, see, e.g.,
[
3
,
6
,
15
,
16
,
37
,
46
,
47
]
.
However, the specific form of parameters in (
77
) comes from the derivations of our convergence analysis, when we enforce a descent direction of an appropriate Lyapunov function (defined later).
This step is rather technical, and we only provide the update rule (
77
) instead of presenting the detailed derivations in this paper.

Remark 8

Note that (
GAEG
) studied in Section
6
can be written into the form (
GAEG+
).
However, the choice of parameters is significantly different.

\mathrm{(b)}

Three special instances.

The generalized method (
GAEG+
) possibly covers a wide class of schemes by instantiating different directions
u^{k}
, which satisfy (
78
).
As before, we consider the following three special choices of
u^{k}
.

\mathrm{(i)}

Variant 1: EG-Type Variant.

If we choose
u^{k}:=Fx^{k}
, then (
78
) automatically holds with
\kappa=\hat{\kappa}=0
.
Thus (
GAEG+
) reduces to

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}x^{k+1}&:=&y^{k}-\eta(\hat{w}^{k+1}-\gamma_{k}w^{k}),\\
y^{k}&:=&x^{k+1}+\theta_{k}(x^{k+1}-x^{k})-\eta_{k}w^{k+1}+\lambda_{k}\hat{w}^{k+1}-\nu_{k}w^{k},\end{array}\right.

(AEG)

where
\gamma_{k}
,
\theta_{k}
,
\eta_{k}
,
\lambda_{k}
, and
\nu_{k}
are updated as in (
77
).
Clearly, (
AEG
) can be viewed as an accelerated extragradient (AEG) method for solving (
NI
).
Note that our method (
AEG
) is different from existing works such as
[
88
]
due to both the update rule and the choice of parameters.
As in any EG method, (
AEG
) requires two evaluations
Fx^{k}
and
Fy^{k}
of
F
, but we only need one resolvent
J_{\eta T}
of
T
at each iteration.

\mathrm{(ii)}

Variant 2: Past-EG-Type Variant.

If we choose
u^{k}:=Fy^{k-1}
, then (
78
) holds with
\kappa=1
and
\hat{\kappa}=0
.
Clearly, (
GAEG+
) reduces to the following variant:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}x^{k+1}&:=&y^{k}-\eta(\hat{w}^{k+1}-\gamma_{k}\hat{w}^{k}),\\
y^{k}&:=&x^{k+1}+\theta_{k}(x^{k+1}-x^{k})-(\eta_{k}-\lambda_{k})\hat{w}^{k+1}-\nu_{k}\hat{w}^{k}.\end{array}\right.

(OG)

This scheme can be viewed as an accelerated [Popov’s] past-extragradient (or equivalently, optimistic gradient) method for solving (
NI
).
Unlike (
AEG
), (
OG
) only requires one evaluation
Fy^{k}
and one resolvent
J_{\eta T}
of
T
at each iteration, saving one evaluation of
F
.

\mathrm{(iii)}

Variant 3: Generalization.

We can construct
u^{k}
as follows:

u^{k}:=\hat{\alpha}d^{k-1}+(1-\alpha)Fx^{k}+\alpha Fy^{k-1}.

(79)

Clearly,
u^{k}
is a linear combination of
Fx^{k}
,
Fy^{k-1}
and
d^{k-1}
for
\alpha,\hat{\alpha}\in\mathbb{R}
.
Then,
u^{k}
satisfies (
78
) with
\kappa=(1+m)\alpha^{2}
and
\hat{\kappa}=(1+m^{-1})\hat{\alpha}^{2}
by Young’s inequality for any
m>0
.
However, to guarantee small
\hat{\kappa}
as in (
78
), we choose
\hat{\alpha}
sufficiently small.
When
\alpha\in(0,1]
, (
GAEG+
) requires two evaluations
Fx^{k}
and
Fy^{k-1}
of
F
and one resolvent
J_{\eta T}
as in (
AEG
).

\mathrm{(c)}

Comparison.

Let us compare our variant (
OG
) with some related works
[
15
,
71
,
88
]
.
If
T=0
, then our scheme (
OG
) reduces to

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}x^{k+1}&:=&y^{k}-\eta(Fy^{k}-\gamma_{k}Fy^{k-1}),\\
y^{k}&:=&x^{k+1}+\theta_{k}(x^{k+1}-x^{k})-(\eta_{k}-\lambda_{k})Fy^{k}-\nu_{k}Fy^{k-1}.\end{array}\right.

(80)

This scheme now solves the equation (
NE
) as
[
15
]
.
However, the
\gamma_{k}
parameter and last term
\nu_{k}Fy^{k-1}
in (
80
) make our variant here different from
(
15
, Algorithm 2)
and allow us to handle the co-hypomonotonicity of
F
instead of the monotonicity of
F
as in
[
15
]
.
In particular, when
\beta=0
in (
77
), (
80
) does not reduce to
(
15
, Algorithm 2)
.
If
T=\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{X}}
, the normal cone of a convex set
\mathcal{X}
, then our variant (
OG
) is also different from
[
71
]
even when we set
\beta=0
.

\mathrm{(d)}

The implementation of (
GAEG+
).

To use the resolvent
J_{\eta T}
of
T
, we can rewrite (
GAEG+
) equivalently to the following scheme:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}x^{k+1}&:=&J_{\eta T}\big(y^{k}-\eta Fy^{k}+\eta\gamma_{k}z^{k}\big),\\
\xi^{k+1}&:=&\frac{1}{\eta}\big(y^{k}-\eta Fy^{k}+\eta\gamma_{k}z^{k}-x^{k+1}\big),\\
z^{k+1}&:=&u^{k+1}+\xi^{k+1},\\
y^{k+1}&:=&x^{k+1}+\theta_{k}(x^{k+1}-x^{k})-\eta_{k}z^{k+1}+\lambda_{k}(Fy^{k}+\xi^{k+1})-\nu_{k}z^{k}.\end{array}\right.

(81)

As before, we assume that
J_{\eta T}
is well-defined and single-valued.

7.2
Key Estimates for Convergence Analysis of (
GAEG+
)

In this subsection, we first establish some key bounds for our convergence analysis of (
GAEG+
) and its instances.
These bounds are independent of the choice of
u^{k}
, and cover all three instances discussed in (i), (ii), and (iii).

\mathrm{(a)}

Auxiliary functions.

First, given
r
,
\mu
,
\eta
,
\beta
, and
t_{k}
in (
77
), let us define the following parameters:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\psi:=\frac{\eta-\beta}{\mu+1},\quad\omega:=\frac{\mu(\eta-\beta)}{\mu+1},\quad\textrm{and}\quad c_{k}:=\big[\psi(t_{k}-r+1)-(r-1)\beta\big]t_{k}.\end{array}

(82)

Next, given
c_{k}
in (
82
), we define the following two functions:

\hskip-8.61108pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{P}_{k}&:=&\|r(x^{k}-x^{\star})+t_{k}(y^{k}-x^{k})\|^{2}+r\mu\|x^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}+2c_{k}\langle z^{k},y^{k}-x^{k}\rangle,\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}_{k}}&:=&\eta^{2}t_{k}^{2}\|d^{k}\|^{2}-t_{k+1}^{2}\|p^{k}\|^{2}+2\eta c_{k}\langle z^{k},d^{k}\rangle+2c_{k+1}\langle z^{k+1},p^{k}\rangle.\end{array}\hskip-8.61108pt

(83)

\mathrm{(b)}

Expanding
\mathcal{P}_{k}-\mathcal{P}_{k+1}
.

We expand
\mathcal{P}_{k}-\mathcal{P}_{k+1}
as in the following lemma.

Lemma 10

Let
\{(x^{k},y^{k})\}
be computed by (
GAEG+
), and
\mathcal{P}_{k}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}_{k}}
be defined by (
76
).
Suppose that the parameters are updated by (
77
) and (
82
).
Then

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{P}_{k}-\mathcal{P}_{k+1}&=&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}_{k}}+\mu(2t_{k}-r-\mu)\|x^{k+1}-x^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}2r[(\eta-\beta)t_{k}-\delta]\langle z^{k+1},x^{k+1}-x^{\star}\rangle\\
&&-{~}2r[(\eta-\beta)t_{k}-\eta(r-1)]\langle z^{k},x^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle\\
&&+{~}2t_{k}\big[\omega(t_{k}-\mu-r)+\mu\eta\big]\langle z^{k+1}-z^{k},x^{k+1}-x^{k}\rangle\\
&&+{~}2\mu\eta t_{k}\langle\hat{w}^{k+1}-z^{k+1},x^{k+1}-x^{k}\rangle.\end{array}

(84)

\mathrm{(c)}

Lower bounding
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}_{k}}
.

First, let us expand
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}_{k}}
defined by (
83
) as follows.

Lemma 11

Under the same setting as in Lemma
10
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}_{k}}
defined by (
83
) can be expressed as

\hskip-4.30554pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}_{k}}&=&\big[(1-\mu)\psi t_{k}-\delta\big][(\eta-\beta)t_{k}-\delta]\|z^{k+1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\big\{\eta(t_{k}-r+1)\big[(\eta-2\psi)(t_{k}-r+1)+2(r-1)\beta\big]-\beta^{2}t_{k}^{2}\big\}\|z^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}2\eta\omega t_{k}^{2}\langle z^{k+1},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle-2\beta\omega t_{k}^{2}\langle z^{k+1},z^{k}\rangle\\
&&-{~}2\eta\omega t_{k}(t_{k}-r+1)\langle\hat{w}^{k+1},z^{k}\rangle.\end{array}\hskip-4.30554pt

(85)

Now, we can lower bound
\mathcal{E}_{k}
as in the following lemma.

Lemma 12

Under the same setting as in Lemma
10
and the
L
-Lipschitz continuity of
F
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}_{k}}
defined by (
83
) satisfies

\begin{array}[]{lcl}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{E}_{k}}&\geq&\Lambda_{k+1}\|z^{k+1}\|^{2}-\Lambda_{k}\|z^{k}\|^{2}+S_{k}\|z^{k+1}\|^{2}+\beta\omega t_{k}^{2}\|z^{k+1}-z^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\eta\omega(1-M^{2}\eta^{2})t_{k}^{2}\|d^{k}\|^{2}+\eta\omega\hat{\phi}t_{k}^{2}\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\eta\omega\phi t_{k}^{2}\|z^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}-\frac{\eta\omega(1+\phi)(1+c_{1})t_{k}^{2}}{c_{1}}\|z^{k+1}-w^{k+1}\|^{2},\end{array}

(86)

where
M^{2}:=\big[(1+\phi)(1+c_{1})+\hat{\phi}\big]L^{2}
for any
\phi\geq 0
,
\hat{\phi}\geq 0
, and
c_{1}>0
, and

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Lambda_{k}&:=&\frac{[(\eta-\beta)(t_{k}-r+1)-(r-1)\beta]^{2}+\mu(r-1)^{2}\eta\beta}{\mu+1},\\
\Gamma&:=&\frac{\mu(r-2)^{2}\eta^{2}+\mu[(\mu-1)r^{2}-2(\mu-3)r+\mu-7]\eta\beta-\mu[\mu r^{2}-2(\mu-1)r+\mu-3]\beta^{2}}{(\mu+1)^{2}},\\
S_{k}&:=&\frac{2\mu(r-2)(\eta-\beta)^{2}}{(\mu+1)^{2}}t_{k}-\Gamma.\end{array}\right.

(87)

7.3
Convergence Analysis of The AEG Method

Let us first establish the convergence of the instance (
AEG
) (the accelerated extragradient scheme) of (
GAEG+
) with
u^{k}:=Fx^{k}
, which is less complicated than the generalization case when
u^{k}\neq Fx^{k}
in (
78
).
We separate this case since we can leverage larger stepsizes and a wider range of
L\rho
(i.e.
L\rho<\frac{1}{2}
).

\mathrm{(a)}

The Lyapunov function and technical lemmas.

Given
c_{k}
in (
82
) and
\Lambda_{k}
in (
87
), consider the following Lyapunov function:

\hskip-8.61108pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{k}&:=&\|r(x^{k}-x^{\star})+t_{k}(y^{k}-x^{k})\|^{2}+r\mu\|x^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}+2c_{k}\langle w^{k},y^{k}-x^{k}\rangle\\
&&+{~}\Lambda_{k}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+2r[(\eta-\beta)t_{k}-\eta(r-1)]\langle w^{k},x^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle.\end{array}\hskip-8.61108pt

(88)

First, we prove the following key bound to establish convergence of (
AEG
).

Lemma 13

For (
NI
), suppose that
\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)\neq\emptyset
,
F
is
L
-Lipschitz continuous, and
\Phi
is
\rho
-co-hypomonotone.
Let
\{(x^{k},y^{k})\}
be generated by (
AEG
)
(
i.e.
u^{k}:=Fx^{k}
)
using the parameters in (
77
) and (
82
).
Let
\mathcal{L}_{k}
be defined by (
88
).
Then

\hskip-8.61108pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{k}-\mathcal{L}_{k+1}&\geq&\mu\big(2t_{k}-r-\mu-\frac{\mu\eta}{\hat{\phi}\omega}\big)\|x^{k+1}-x^{k}\|^{2}+\eta\omega(1-M^{2}\eta^{2})t_{k}^{2}\|d^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}t_{k}\big[\omega(\beta-2\rho)t_{k}+2\rho(\mu\omega+r\omega-\mu\eta)\big]\|w^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\big[S_{k}-2\rho\omega r(r-2)\big]\|w^{k+1}\|^{2}.\end{array}\hskip-8.61108pt

(89)

Next, we lower bound the Lyapunov function
\mathcal{L}_{k}
in (
88
) as follows.

Lemma 14

Under the same setting as in Lemma
13
, we have

\hskip-8.61108pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{k}&\geq&\|r(x^{k}-x^{\star})+t_{k}(y^{k}-x^{k})+[\psi(t_{k}-r+1)-(r-1)\beta]w^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\sigma_{k}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+r\mu\|x^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2},\end{array}\hskip-8.61108pt

(90)

where
\sigma_{k}=\mu\big[\psi(t_{k}-r+1)-r\rho\big]^{2}-\mu\big[r^{2}\rho^{2}-\frac{(r-1)^{2}\beta(\eta-\beta)}{\mu+1}\big]
.

Moreover, we also have

\hskip-8.61108pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{0}&\leq&(C_{0}+r^{2}+r\mu)\|x^{0}-x^{\star}\|^{2}+(C_{0}+\Lambda_{0})\|w^{0}\|^{2},\end{array}\hskip-8.61108pt

(91)

where
C_{0}:=r[(\eta-\beta)t_{0}-\eta(r-1)]
provided that
t_{0}\geq\frac{\eta(r-1)}{\eta-\beta}
.

\mathrm{(b)}

The
\mathcal{O}\left(1/k\right)
-last iterate convergence rates and summable bounds.

For given
\hat{\Gamma}:=\frac{2\Gamma}{r-2}+4\rho\omega r
and
\hat{\phi}:=\frac{1-L^{2}\eta^{2}}{2L^{2}\eta^{2}}>0
, we define

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}&:=&(C_{0}+r^{2}+r\mu)\|x^{0}-x^{\star}\|^{2}+(C_{0}+\Lambda_{0})\|w^{0}\|^{2},\\
t_{0}&:=&\max\Big\{\frac{r+1}{2}+\frac{\eta}{2\hat{\phi}\omega},\ \frac{\hat{\Gamma}}{(\eta-\beta)^{2}},\ \frac{4\rho[\eta-\omega(r+1)]}{\omega(\beta-2\rho)},\ \frac{\eta(r-1)}{\eta-\beta}\Big\},\end{array}

(92)

where
C_{0}:=r[(\eta-\beta)t_{0}-\eta(r-1)]\geq 0
.

Now, we are ready to prove the convergence of (
AEG
) as follows.

Theorem 7.1

For (
NI
), suppose that
\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)\neq\emptyset
,
F
is
L
-Lipschitz continuous, and
\Phi
is
\rho
-co-hypomonotone such that
2L\rho<1
.
Let
\{(x^{k},y^{k})\}
be generated by (
AEG
)
(
i.e.
u^{k}:=Fx^{k}
)
using the parameters in (
77
) and (
82
) with
\mu:=1
.
Moreover, we choose
r>2
, and
t_{k}
,
\beta
,
\eta
, and
\hat{\phi}
such that

\begin{array}[]{ll}t_{k}:=k+t_{0},\quad 2\rho<\beta<\eta<\frac{1}{L},\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{\phi}:=\frac{1-L^{2}\eta^{2}}{2L^{2}\eta^{2}}>0.\end{array}

(93)

Then, the following summable bounds hold:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\big(2k+2t_{0}-r-1-\frac{\eta}{\hat{\phi}\omega}\big)\|x^{k+1}-x^{k}\|^{2}&\leq&\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2},\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\eta\omega(1-L^{2}\eta^{2})(k+t_{0})^{2}\|d^{k}\|^{2}&\leq&2\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2},\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\big[(\eta-\beta)^{2}(k+t_{0})-\hat{\Gamma}\big]\|w^{k+1}\|^{2}&\leq&\frac{2}{r-2}\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2},\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+t_{0})^{2}\|w^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}&\leq&\frac{2}{\omega(\beta-2\rho)}\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2},\end{array}

(94)

where
\omega:=\frac{\eta-\beta}{2}>0
,
\hat{\Gamma}:=\frac{2\Gamma}{r-2}+4\rho\omega r
, and
\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}
and
t_{0}
are given in (
92
).

Furthermore, we also have

\|Fx^{k}+\xi^{k}\|^{2}\leq\frac{4\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}}{(\eta-\beta)^{2}(k+t_{0}-r+1)^{2}},\quad\text{where}\quad\xi^{k}\in Tx^{k}.

(95)

Remark 9

We highlight that we can extend our results in Theorem
7.1
to cover the extreme cases
\eta:=\frac{1}{L}
and
\beta:=2\rho
.
However, the analysis is different and thus we omit it here to avoid overloading the paper.

\mathrm{(c)}

The
o\left(1/k\right)
-convergence rates.

Next, we establish
o\left(1/k\right)
rates of (
AEG
).

Theorem 7.2

Under the same conditions and settings as in Theorem
7.1
, we have the following results:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\lim_{k\to\infty}k^{2}\|Fx^{k}+\xi^{k}\|^{2}=0,\\
\lim_{k\to\infty}k^{2}\|x^{k+1}-x^{k}\|^{2}=0,\\
\lim_{k\to\infty}k^{2}\|y^{k}-x^{k}\|^{2}=0.\end{array}

(96)

These expressions show that
\|Fx^{k}+\xi^{k}\|=o\left(1/k\right)
,
\|x^{k+1}-x^{k}\|=o\left(1/k\right)
, and
\|y^{k}-x^{k}\|=o\left(1/k\right)
, respectively for (
AEG
), where
\xi^{k}\in Tx^{k}
.

\mathrm{(d)}

The convergence of iterate sequences.

Finally, we prove the convergence of
\{x^{k}\}
and also
\{y^{k}\}
to a solution
x^{\star}\in\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)
.

Theorem 7.3

Under the same conditions and settings as in Theorem
7.1
, if, additionally,
T
is closed
(
in particular,
T
is maximally monotone
)
, then both
\{x^{k}\}
and
\{y^{k}\}
generated by (
AEG
) converge to
x^{\star}\in\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)
.

Remark 10

Theorem
7.3
requires
T
to be closed.
Clearly, if
T
is maximally monotone, then by
(
9
, Proposition 20.37)
,
T
is closed.
If
T
is upper semi-continuous on
\mathrm{dom}(T)
and has closed values, then
T
is also closed
[
38
]
.

7.4
Convergence Analysis of The Generalized AEG Method

In this subsection, we will investigate the convergence of the generalized scheme (
GAEG+
) under the condition (
78
).
Our analysis is new and requires several additional technical steps compared to Subsection
7.3
.

\mathrm{(a)}

Technical lemmas.

Since
z^{k}
can be different from
w^{k}
, our first step is to process the product term
\langle z^{k+1}-z^{k},x^{k+1}-x^{k}\rangle
from (
84
).

Lemma 15

Denote
a_{k}:=t_{k}\big[\omega(t_{k}-r-\mu)+\mu\eta\big]
.
We consider the quantity:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{F}_{k}&:=&2a_{k}\langle z^{k+1}-z^{k},x^{k+1}-x^{k}\rangle.\end{array}

(97)

Then, for any
\Delta>0
,
r>2
,
\mu\geq 1
, and
\eta\geq\frac{(r+\mu-1)\beta}{r-2}
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{F}_{k}&\geq&2a_{k+1}\langle z^{k+1}-w^{k+1},y^{k+1}-x^{k+1}\rangle-2a_{k}\langle z^{k}-w^{k},y^{k}-x^{k}\rangle\\
&&-{~}2\omega t_{k}[(\eta+\beta)t_{k}+\delta]\|z^{k+1}-w^{k+1}\|^{2}-\frac{\eta\omega t_{k}^{2}}{2}\|z^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{\beta\omega t_{k}^{2}}{2}\|z^{k+1}-z^{k}\|^{2}-\frac{\delta\omega t_{k}}{2}\|z^{k+1}\|^{2}-\frac{\eta\omega t_{k}^{2}}{\Delta r}\|d^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\Delta\eta r\omega t_{k}^{2}\|z^{k}-w^{k}\|^{2}+2a_{k}\langle w^{k+1}-w^{k},x^{k+1}-x^{k}\rangle\\
&&+{~}2b_{k}\langle z^{k+1}-w^{k+1},x^{k+1}-x^{k}\rangle,\end{array}

(98)

where
b_{k}:=\omega(r+\mu-1)(t_{k}-r-\mu)+\mu\eta(r+\mu)
.

Now, we combine Lemmas
10
,
12
, and
15
to prove the following result.

Lemma 16

Suppose that
\Phi
is
\rho
-co-hypomonotone and
F
is
L
-Lipschitz continuous.
Let
\{(x^{k},y^{k})\}
be generated by (
GAEG+
) using the update rules (
77
) and (
82
).
Let us consider the following function:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{G}_{k}&:=&\Lambda_{k}\|z^{k}\|^{2}+\|r(x^{k}-x^{\star})+t_{k}(y^{k}-x^{k})\|^{2}+r\mu\|x^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}2r\big[(\eta-\beta)t_{k}-\eta(r-1)\big]\langle z^{k},x^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle\\
&&+{~}2c_{k}\langle z^{k},y^{k}-x^{k}\rangle+2a_{k}\langle z^{k}-w^{k},y^{k}-x^{k}\rangle.\end{array}

(99)

Then, for any
\Delta>0
,
r>2
,
\mu\geq 1
, and
\eta\geq\frac{(r+\mu-1)\beta}{r-2}
, we have

\hskip-8.61108pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{G}_{k}-\mathcal{G}_{k+1}&\geq&\big[S_{k}-\frac{\delta\omega t_{k}}{2}-4\rho\omega r(r-2)\big]\|z^{k+1}\|^{2}-\frac{\omega r^{2}(r-2)^{2}}{\eta t_{k}^{2}}\|x^{k+1}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\mu\big(t_{k}-r-\mu-\frac{2\mu\eta}{\omega}\big)\|x^{k+1}-x^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\eta\omega\big(1-\frac{1}{\Delta r}-M^{2}\eta^{2}\big)t_{k}^{2}\|d^{k}\|^{2}+\eta\omega\hat{\phi}t_{k}^{2}\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\eta\omega(\phi-1)t_{k}^{2}\|z^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}-\big(\Delta r\eta+8\rho\big)\omega t_{k}^{2}\|z^{k}-w^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\hat{\Theta}_{k}\|z^{k+1}-w^{k+1}\|^{2}+\frac{(\beta-8\rho)\omega t_{k}^{2}}{2}\|z^{k+1}-z^{k}\|^{2},\end{array}\hskip-12.91663pt

(100)

where
\hat{\Theta}_{k}:=\omega t_{k}\big[(\eta(5+2\phi)+2\beta+8\rho)t_{k}+2\delta+\omega(r+\mu-1)^{2}\big]+4\rho\omega r(r-2)
.

\mathrm{(b)}

The Lyapunov function and its descent property.

Now, we define the following Lyapunov function to analyze our generalized scheme (
GAEG+
):

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{k}&:=&\|r(x^{k}-x^{\star})+t_{k}(y^{k}-x^{k})\|^{2}+r\mu\|x^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}+\Lambda_{k}\|z^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}2r\big[(\eta-\beta)t_{k}-\eta(r-1)\big]\langle z^{k},x^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle\\
&&+{~}2c_{k}\langle z^{k},y^{k}-x^{k}\rangle+2a_{k}\langle z^{k}-w^{k},y^{k}-x^{k}\rangle+\alpha_{k}\|z^{k}-w^{k}\|^{2},\end{array}

(101)

where
\alpha_{k}:=(\Delta r\eta+8\rho)\omega t_{k}^{2}
for some
\Delta>0
determined later,
c_{k}
is in (
82
), and
\Lambda_{k}
is in (
87
).
We prove the following result.

Lemma 17

For (
NI
), suppose that
\Phi
is
\rho
-co-hypomonotone and
F
is
L
-Lipschitz continuous.
Let
\{(x^{k},y^{k})\}
be generated by (
GAEG+
) using the update rules (
77
) and (
82
) and
u^{k}
satisfying (
78
).
Let
\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{k}
be defined by (
101
).
Given
r>2
,
\mu\geq 1
,
\epsilon\geq 0
, and
\hat{\epsilon}\geq 0
, we choose
\beta
,
\eta
, and
t_{k}
such that

\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta:=8\rho+2\epsilon,\quad\frac{(r+\mu-1)\beta}{r-2}\leq\eta,\quad\text{and}\quad t_{k}\geq\bar{t}_{0}.\end{array}

(102)

where
\bar{t}_{0}:=\frac{D+\sqrt{D^{2}+4\eta E}}{2\eta}
with
D:=2\delta+\omega(r+\mu-1)^{2}+2\Delta r\eta+2\beta+2{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\epsilon}\eta}
and
E:=4\rho r(r-2)+\Delta r\eta+8\rho+\hat{\epsilon}\eta
.
Then, we have

\hskip-8.61108pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{k}-\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{k+1}&\geq&\frac{\omega[3(r-2)\psi-(r-1)\beta]t_{k}-\hat{\Gamma}}{2}\|z^{k+1}\|^{2}-\frac{\omega r^{2}(r-2)^{2}}{\eta t_{k}^{2}}\|x^{k+1}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\eta\omega(\hat{\phi}-\kappa\Theta)t_{k}^{2}\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\mu\big(t_{k}-r-\mu-\frac{2\mu\eta}{\omega}\big)\|x^{k+1}-x^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\eta\omega\big(1-\frac{1}{\Delta r}-\hat{\kappa}\Theta-M^{2}\eta^{2}\big)t_{k}^{2}\|d^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\hat{\epsilon}\omega\eta t_{k+1}^{2}\|z^{k+1}-w^{k+1}\|^{2}+\epsilon\omega t_{k}^{2}\|z^{k+1}-z^{k}\|^{2},\end{array}\hskip-8.61108pt

(103)

where
\hat{\Gamma}:=2\Gamma+8\rho\omega r(r-2)
and
\Theta:=10+2\phi+\Delta r+\hat{\epsilon}
.

\mathrm{(c)}

The lower bound of
\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{k}
.

We also need to lower bound
\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{k}
as follows.

Lemma 18

Under the same setting as in Lemma
17
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{k}&\geq&r(\mu-1)\|x^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}+\frac{(\eta-\beta)^{2}t_{k}^{2}}{2(\mu+1)^{2}}\|z^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\big(\frac{\Delta r\eta}{\omega}-4r-1\big)\omega^{2}t_{k}^{2}\|z^{k}-w^{k}\|^{2},\end{array}

(104)

provided that
\mu\geq 1
,
r>2
,
t_{k}\geq r
, and
t_{k}\geq\hat{t}_{0}
, in which
\hat{t}_{0}
is defined by

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\hat{t}_{0}&:=&\begin{cases}\frac{B+\sqrt{B^{2}-AC}}{A},&\text{if }B^{2}\geq AC,\\
0,&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}\end{array}

(105)

where

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}A&:=&\frac{(\eta-\beta)^{2}}{(\mu+1)^{2}}\left(\mu-\frac{1}{2r}-\frac{1}{2}\right)>0,\\
B&:=&\frac{(\eta-\beta)}{(\mu+1)^{2}}\big[\frac{(r-1)[2r\mu(\eta-\beta)-(\eta+\mu\beta)]}{2r}+\rho(\mu+1)(2r\mu-1)\big],\\
C&:=&(r-1)\big\{\frac{(r-1)(\eta+\mu\beta)}{(\mu+1)^{2}}\big[\left(\mu-\frac{1}{2r}\right)\eta-\frac{(2r+1)\mu\beta}{2r}\big]-2\rho[\eta-(2r+1)\omega]\big\}.\end{array}\right.

In particular, if we choose
\Delta:=3
, then we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{k}\geq r(\mu-1)\|x^{k}-x^{\star}\|^{2}+\frac{(\eta-\beta)^{2}t_{k}^{2}}{4(\mu+1)^{2}}\|w^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

(106)

\mathrm{(d)}

The
\mathcal{O}\left(1/k\right)
convergence rates and summable bounds.

Fix
r>2
and
\hat{\epsilon}\geq 0
, suppose that the constant
\hat{\kappa}
in (
78
) satisfies

\begin{array}[]{lcl}0\leq\hat{\kappa}<\frac{3r-1}{3r(12+3r+\hat{\epsilon})}.\end{array}

(107)

Given
\hat{\kappa}
as in (
107
),
\bar{t}_{0}
defined in Lemma
17
, and
\hat{t}_{0}
defined by (
105
), we denote the following two constants:

\hskip-8.61108pt\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Psi&:=&\frac{1}{\sqrt{4+12\kappa+3\kappa r+\kappa\hat{\epsilon}}}\big[1-\frac{1}{3r}-\hat{\kappa}(12+3r+\hat{\epsilon})\big]\in(0,1],\\
t_{0}&:=&\max\Big\{\bar{t}_{0},\ \hat{t}_{0},\ r,\ \frac{\eta(r-1)}{\eta-\beta},\ \frac{\mu}{\omega}(r-1)[\eta-(2r+1)\omega],\ \frac{(r-2)\sqrt{\omega r}}{\sqrt{\eta(\mu-1)}}\Big\}.\end{array}\right.\hskip-8.61108pt

(108)

Note that if
\hat{\kappa}=0
, (e.g., when
u^{k}:=Fy^{k-1}
as in (
OG
), then (
107
) automatically holds, and
\Psi
in (
108
) reduces to
\Psi:=\frac{3r-1}{3r\sqrt{4+\kappa(12+3r+\hat{\epsilon})}}
.

Now, we are ready to prove the convergence of (
GAEG+
) as follows.

Theorem 7.4

For (
NI
), suppose that
\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)\neq\emptyset
,
\Phi
is
\rho
-co-hypomonotone, and
F
is
L
-Lipschitz continuous.
Fix
r>2
and
\mu>1
, let
\{(x^{k},y^{k})\}
be generated by (
GAEG+
) using the update rules (
77
) and (
82
) and
u^{k}
such that (
78
) holds with
\hat{\kappa}
as in (
107
).
Suppose that
L\rho<\frac{(r-2)\Psi}{8(r+\mu-1)}
for
\Psi
given by (
108
).
Let us choose
t_{k}
,
\beta
, and
\eta
such that

\begin{array}[]{ll}&t_{k}=k+t_{0},\quad\beta:=8\rho+2\epsilon,\ \ \text{and}\ \ \frac{(r+\mu-1)\beta}{r-2}\leq\eta\leq\bar{\eta}:=\frac{\Psi}{L},\end{array}

(109)

where
t_{0}
is given in (
108
) and
\epsilon\geq 0
is given.
Then, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+t_{0})\|x^{k+1}-x^{k}\|^{2}&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+t_{0})^{2}\|z^{k}-w^{k}\|^{2}&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+t_{0})^{2}\|z^{k+1}-z^{k}\|^{2}&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(\bar{\eta}^{2}-\eta^{2})(k+t_{0})^{2}\|d^{k}\|^{2}&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+t_{0})\|z^{k}\|^{2}&<&+\infty,\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+t_{0})\|w^{k}\|^{2}&<&+\infty.\end{array}

(110)

Moreover, for
\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}
given in (
92
), we also have

\|Fx^{k}+\xi^{k}\|^{2}\leq\frac{4\Omega(\mu+1)^{2}\mathcal{R}_{0}^{2}}{(\eta-8\rho)^{2}(k+t_{0})^{2}},\quad\textrm{where}\quad\xi^{k}\in Tx^{k},

(111)

where
\Omega:=\left(\frac{1+t_{0}+c}{1+t_{0}-c}\right)^{\frac{c}{2}}\exp\left(\frac{c^{2}}{(1+t_{0})^{2}-c^{2}}\right)<\infty
with
c^{2}:=\frac{\omega r(r-2)^{2}}{\eta(\mu-1)}
.

\mathrm{(e)}

The
o\left(1/k\right)
last-iterate convergence rates.

Our next step is to establish
o\left(1/k\right)
convergence rates of (
GAEG+
).

Theorem 7.5

Under the same conditions and settings as in Theorem
7.4
and we additionally choose
\eta<\bar{\eta}
, then we have the following limits:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\lim_{k\to\infty}k^{2}\|Fx^{k}+\xi^{k}\|^{2}=0,\\
\lim_{k\to\infty}k^{2}\|x^{k+1}-x^{k}\|^{2}=0,\\
\lim_{k\to\infty}k^{2}\|y^{k}-x^{k}\|^{2}=0.\end{array}

(112)

These expressions show that
\|Fx^{k}+\xi^{k}\|=o\left(1/k\right)
,
\|x^{k+1}-x^{k}\|=o\left(1/k\right)
, and
\|y^{k}-x^{k}\|=o\left(1/k\right)
, respectively for (
GAEG+
), where
\xi^{k}\in Tx^{k}
.

\mathrm{(f)}

The convergence of iterate sequences.

Finally, we prove the convergence of the iterate sequences
\{x^{k}\}
and
\{y^{k}\}
generated by (
GAEG+
) to a solution
x^{\star}\in\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)
.
The proof of this result is rather involved and hence, we divide it into several technical lemmas (see Appendix
F
).

Theorem 7.6

Under the same conditions and settings as in Theorem
7.4
, if we additionally choose
\eta<\bar{\eta}
and assume that
T
is closed, then both
\{x^{k}\}
and
\{y^{k}\}
generated by (
GAEG+
) converge to
x^{\star}\in\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)
.

Remark 11

We have used several times of Young’s inequality in our analysis in Subsection
7.4
.
Our analysis as well as the ranges of
L\rho
and other parameters can be improved by appropriately choosing the constant
c
whenever we applying Young’s inequality of the form
\|u-v\|^{2}\leq(1+c)\|u\|^{2}+\frac{1+c}{c}\|v\|^{2}
.

8
Numerical Experiments

In this section, we provide a number of experiments to validate our algorithms and their theoretical results.
All the algorithms are implemented in Python running on a single node of a Linux server (called
Longleaf
) with the configuration: AMD EPYC 7713 64-Core Processor, 512KB cache, and 64GB RAM.

8.1
Mathematical model: Quadratic minimax optimization

The mathematical model we use for our experiments is the following simplex constrained quadratic minimax problem:

\displaystyle\min_{u\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}}}\max_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}}}\Big\{\mathcal{L}(u,v)=f(u)+\mathcal{H}(x,y)-g(v)\Big\},

(113)

where
\mathcal{H}(x,y):=\frac{1}{2}u^{\top}Au+b^{\top}u+u^{\top}Lv-\frac{1}{2}v^{\top}Bv-c^{\top}v
such that
A\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{1}}
and
B\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}\times p_{2}}
are symmetric matrices,
b\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}}
,
c\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}}
are given vectors, and
L\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}}
is a given matrix.
The functions
f(u)=\delta_{\Delta_{p_{1}}}(u)
and
g(v)=\delta_{\Delta_{p_{2}}}(v)
are added to handle simplex constraints
u\in\Delta_{p_{1}}
and
v\in\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p_{2}}}
, where
\Delta_{p_{1}}
and
\Delta_{p_{2}}
are the standard simplexes in
\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}}
and
\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}}
, respectively, and
\delta_{\mathcal{X}}
is the indicator function of a closed convex set
\mathcal{X}
.

First, we denote
x\coloneqq[u,v]\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
for
p:=p_{1}+p_{2}
, which is the concatenation of the primal variable
u\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}}
and its dual variable
v\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}}
.
Next, we define
\mathbf{F}\coloneqq\left[[A,L],[-L^{\top},B]\right]
as the KKT (Karush-Kuhn-Tucker) matrix in
\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
constructed from the four blocks
A,L,-L^{\top}
, and
B
, and
\mathbf{f}\coloneqq[b,c]\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
.
The operator
F:\mathbb{R}^{p}\to\mathbb{R}^{p}
is then defined as
Fx\coloneqq\mathbf{F}x+\mathbf{f}
.
Then, we denote
T\coloneqq[\partial\delta_{\Delta_{p_{1}}},\partial\delta_{\Delta_{p_{2}}}]
the maximally monotone mapping constructed from the subdifferentials of
\delta_{\Delta_{p_{1}}}
and
\delta_{\Delta_{p_{2}}}
.
Finally, the optimality condition of (
113
) becomes
0\in Fx+Tx
covered by (
NI
).

8.2
Numerical experiments

\mathrm{(a)}

Data generation.

Our aim is to verify the proposed algorithms and their corresponding theoretical results.
In what follows, all matrices and vectors in
F
are generated randomly from the standard normal distribution.
We generate the matrix
A=QDQ^{\top}
, where
Q
is an orthonormal matrix obtained from the QR factorization of a random matrix, and
D=\mathrm{diag}(d_{1},\dots,d_{p_{1}})
is the diagonal matrix formed from
d_{1},\dots,d_{p_{1}}
randomly generated and then clipped by a lower bound
\underline{d}
, i.e.
d_{j}\coloneqq\max\{d_{j},\underline{d}\}
.
The matrix
B
is also generated by the same way.
The matrix
L
and vectors
b
and
c
are also randomly generated.

\mathrm{(b)}

Experiment setup.

We perform four different experiments.
In
Experiment 1
and
Experiment 2
, we choose
\underline{d}=0.1
(monotone), and run the four sets of algorithms on 10 problem instances for each case:
p=1000
and
p=2000
, respectively.
In
Experiment 3
and
Experiment 4
, we choose
\underline{d}=-10^{-3}
(possibly nonmonotone) and run the same four sets of algorithms on 10 problem instances for each case:
p=1000
and
p=2000
, respectively.
Then, we report the mean of the relative operator norm
\frac{\|\mathcal{G}_{\eta}x^{k}\|}{\|\mathcal{G}_{\eta}x^{0}\|}
over 10 problem instances, where
\mathcal{G}_{\eta}\coloneqq\eta^{-1}\left(x-J_{\eta T}(x-\eta Fx)\right)
is defined in (
4
).

\mathrm{(c)}

Algorithms and parameters.

In each
Experiment
, we examine the following four sets of algorithms.
In the first set, we consider five variants using the direction
u^{k}:=Fx^{k}
of (
GEAG
), (
GFEG
), (
GFEG+
), (
GAEG
), and (
GAEG+
).
Next, in the second one, we consider five variants of (
GEAG
), (
GFEG
), (
GFEG+
), (
GAEG
), and (
GAEG+
) using
u^{k}:=Fy^{k-1}
(in fact, (
GFEG
) uses a slightly different direction
u^{k}=Fy^{k-1}+\xi^{k}-\xi^{k-1}
due to the choice
\alpha=0
,
\hat{\alpha}=1
).
Then, in the third set, we investigate different variants of (
GFEG+
) by fixing
r=3
and choosing different values of
\mu
.
Finally, in the fourth set, we study different variants of (
GAEG+
) by modifying the choice of its corresponding parameters.
The stepsize of each algorithm is tuned manually to obtain the best possible performance for all algorithms, but not necessarily optimal.
The starting points are always chosen as
x^{0}\coloneqq 0.01\cdot\texttt{ones}(p)
.

\mathrm{(d)}

Results.

The numerical results of
Experiment 1
and
Experiment 2
are reported in Figures
2
,
3
,
4
, and
5
.

Figure 2:
The behaviors of the first set of algorithms for solving (
NI
) in
Experiments 1
and
2
when we choose
u^{k}:=Fx^{k}
.
The plot reveals the mean of 10 problem instances.

NI
) in
Experiments 1
and
2
when we choose
u^{k}:=Fy^{k-1}
.
The plot reveals the mean of 10 problem instances.

Figure 4:
The behaviors of (
GFEG+
) for solving (
NI
) in
Experiments 1
and
2
.
The plot reveals the mean of 10 problem instances.
The legend presents the values of
\mu
used in the corresponding instant of (
GFEG+
).

Figure 5:
The behaviors of (
GAEG+
) for solving (
NI
) in
Experiments 1
and
2
.
The plot reveals the mean of 10 problem instances.
The legend presents the values of
r
and
\mu
used in the corresponding instant of (
GAEG+
). For example,
(3,1)
means the solid orange line with filled circle marker corresponds to the instance of (
GAEG+
) using
r=3
and
\mu=1
.

From Figure
2
, we can see that in both experiments, (
GEAG
), (
GFEG
), and (
GAEG
) have a similar performance within the accuracy of
10^{-4}
after 5000 iterations.
Our (
GFEG+
) gives a better performance than these three schemes, but (
GAEG+
) strongly outperforms its competitors when providing the accuracy of
10^{-7}
after the same number of iterations.
In fact, other experiments reveal that (
GAEG+
) can reach the accuracy of
10^{-14}
after
5000
iterations by appropriately tuning the parameters (see Figure
5
).

A similar observation can also be seen from Figure
3
, which presents the behaviors of the second set of algorithms using
u^{k}:=Fy^{k-1}
.
However, (
GFEG+
) now performs better and (
GAEG+
) is slightly slowed down.

Next, we test (
GFEG+
) with
u^{k}:=Fx^{k}
by fixing
r=3
and choosing
6
different values of
\mu
ranging from
0.05
to
0.05
.
Figure
4
shows the results of the two experiments, where we observe that when
\mu
is decreasing, this method converges faster and achieves better accuracy.

Finally, in Figure
5
, we fixed the values of
\eta
and
\beta
and examine the effect of
r
and
\mu
to the performance of (
GAEG+
). The numerical results show that larger values of
r
and
\mu
can significantly improve the performance of (
GAEG+
), which helps this algorithm become more effective and outperform the remaining competitors.

\mathrm{(e)}

Experiments with nonmonotone problems.

We also perform a similar test with four sets of experiments as in
Experiment 1
and
Experiment 2
, but on a class of possibly nonmonotone problem instances of (
113
).
We denote these experiments by
Experiment 3
and
Experiment 4
.

Figure 6:
The behaviors of the first set of algorithms for solving (
NI
) in
Experiment 3
and
Experiment 4
.
The plot reveals the mean of 10 problem instances.

Figure 7:
The behaviors of the second set of algorithms for solving (
NI
) in
Experiment 3
and
Experiment 4
.
The plot reveals the mean of 10 problem instances.

Figure 8:
The behaviors of (
GFEG+
) for solving (
NI
) in
Experiments 3
and
4
.
The plot reveals the mean of 10 problem instances.
The legend presents the values of
\mu
used in the corresponding instant of (
GFEG+
).

Figure 9:
The behaviors of (
GAEG+
) for solving (
NI
) in
Experiments 3
and
4
.
The plot reveals the mean of 10 problem instances.
The legend presents the values of
r
and
\mu
used in the corresponding instant of (
GAEG+
).
For example,
(3,1)
means the solid orange line with filled circle marker corresponds to the instance of (
GAEG+
) using
r=3
and
\mu=1
.

Despite the relaxation of the monotonicity of the involved operator, the results presented in Figures
6
,
7
,
8
, and
9
are still consistent with what were observed in the monotone scenarios in
Experiment 1
and
Experiment 2
.
These findings reinforce the reliability of the accelerated extragradient-type methods studied in this paper across diverse circumstances and futher establish their potential for applications where monotonicity is not guaranteed.

Appendix A
Appendix 1: Useful Technical Lemmas

We need the following lemmas for our convergence analysis in the paper.

Lemma 19
(see Lemma 5.31,
[
9
]
)

Let
\{u_{k}\}
,
\{v_{k}\}
, and
\{\gamma_{k}\}
be nonnegative sequences in
\mathbb{R}
such that

u_{k+1}\leq(1+\gamma_{k})u_{k}-v_{k},\quad\forall k\geq 0.

(114)

If
A:=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\gamma_{k}<+\infty
and
\omega:=\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}(1+\gamma_{k})\in[1,+\infty)
, then

\begin{array}[]{lcl}u_{k+1}\leq u_{0}\prod_{l=0}^{k}(1+\gamma_{l})+\sum_{l=0}^{k}\varepsilon_{l}\prod_{j=l+1}^{k}(1+\gamma_{j})\leq\omega(u_{0}+B_{k}).\end{array}\vskip-2.15277pt

(115)

Moreover,
\{u_{k}\}
converges
(
i.e.
\lim_{k\to\infty}u_{k}
exists
)
and

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}v_{k}&\leq&(1+\omega A)u_{0}.\end{array}

(116)

Lemma 20
(see
[
82
]
)

For any
r>0
and
c>0
, let
A_{k}:=\sum_{l=0}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}k}}\frac{1}{(l+r)^{2}}
and
\omega_{k}:=\prod_{l=0}^{k}\big(1+\frac{c}{(l+r)^{2}}\big)
.
Then, we have
A_{k}\leq\frac{1}{r}
and
\omega_{k}\leq e^{c/r}
for
k\geq 0
.

Appendix B
The Proof of Technical Results in Section
3

This section provides the full proof of Lemmas and Theorems in Section
3
.

B.1
The Proof of Lemma
1

Since
T
is maximally
3
-cyclically monotone, for any
\xi^{k+1}\in Tx^{k+1}
,
\xi^{k}\in Tx^{k}
, and
\zeta^{k}\in Ty^{k}
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\langle\xi^{k+1},x^{k+1}-x^{k}\rangle+\langle\xi^{k},x^{k}-y^{k}\rangle+\langle\zeta^{k},y^{k}-x^{k+1}\rangle\geq 0.\end{array}

By the monotonicity of
F
, we also have
\langle Fx^{k+1}-Fx^{k},x^{k+1}-x^{k}\rangle\geq 0
.
Summing up this inequality and the last one, and then using
w^{k+1}=Fx^{k+1}+\xi^{k+1}
,
w^{k}:=Fx^{k}+\xi^{k}
, and
\tilde{w}^{k}:=Fx^{k}+\zeta^{k}
from (
8
), we obtain

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\langle w^{k+1},x^{k+1}-x^{k}\rangle-\langle\tilde{w}^{k},x^{k+1}-x^{k}\rangle+\langle w^{k}-\tilde{w}^{k},x^{k}-y^{k}\rangle\geq 0.\end{array}

(117)

Now, let us denote
e^{k}:=u^{k}-Fx^{k}=\tilde{z}^{k}-\tilde{w}^{k}
.
Then, we get
\tilde{z}^{k}=\tilde{w}^{k}+e^{k}
.
By this representation, we can derive from (
9
) that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}x^{k+1}-x^{k}&=&-\frac{\tau_{k}}{1-\tau_{k}}(x^{k+1}-x^{0})-\frac{\eta}{1-\tau_{k}}\hat{w}^{k+1},\\
x^{k+1}-x^{k}&=&-\tau_{k}(x^{k}-x^{0})-\eta\hat{w}^{k+1},\\
x^{k}-y^{k}&=&\tau_{k}(x^{k}-x^{0})+\hat{\eta}_{k}\tilde{w}^{k}+\hat{\eta}_{k}e^{k}.\end{array}\right.

Substituting these relations into (
117
), then using Young’s inequality in
{\tiny\textcircled{1}}
for any
r>0
, and rearranging terms, we arrive at

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{T}_{[1]}&:=&\tau_{k}\langle w^{k},x^{k}-x^{0}\rangle-\frac{\tau_{k}}{1-\tau_{k}}\langle w^{k+1},x^{k+1}-x^{0}\rangle\\
&\geq&\frac{\eta}{1-\tau_{k}}\langle w^{k+1},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle-\eta\langle\tilde{w}^{k},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle-\hat{\eta}_{k}\langle w^{k},\tilde{w}^{k}\rangle+\hat{\eta}_{k}\|\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\hat{\eta}_{k}\langle e^{k},w^{k}-\tilde{w}^{k}\rangle\\
&\overset{\tiny{\tiny\textcircled{1}}}{\geq}&\frac{\eta}{1-\tau_{k}}\langle w^{k+1},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle-\eta\langle\tilde{w}^{k},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle-\hat{\eta}_{k}\langle w^{k},\tilde{w}^{k}\rangle+\hat{\eta}_{k}\|\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{r\hat{\eta}_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}-\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2}-\frac{\hat{\eta}_{k}}{2r}\|e^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Multiplying this inequality by
\frac{b_{k}}{\tau_{k}}
and using
b_{k+1}=\frac{b_{k}}{1-\tau_{k}}
from (
12
), we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{T}_{[2]}&:=&b_{k}\langle w^{k},x^{k}-x^{0}\rangle-b_{k+1}\langle w^{k+1},x^{k+1}-x^{0}\rangle\\
&\geq&\frac{\eta b_{k+1}}{\tau_{k}}\langle w^{k+1}-\tilde{w}^{k},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle+\eta b_{k+1}\langle\tilde{w}^{k},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle-\frac{b_{k}\hat{\eta}_{k}}{\tau_{k}}\langle w^{k},\tilde{w}^{k}\rangle\\
&&+{~}\frac{b_{k}\hat{\eta}_{k}}{\tau_{k}}\|\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2}-\frac{rb_{k}\hat{\eta}_{k}}{2\tau_{k}}\|w^{k}-\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2}-\frac{b_{k}\hat{\eta}_{k}}{2r\tau_{k}}\|e^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

(118)

Next, from (
9
), we have
x^{k+1}-y^{k}=-\eta\hat{w}^{k+1}+\hat{\eta}_{k}\tilde{z}^{k}=-\eta\hat{w}^{k+1}+\hat{\eta}_{k}\tilde{w}^{k}+\hat{\eta}_{k}e^{k}
.
Using this expression, the
L
-Lipschitz continuity of
F
, and Young’s inequality in
{\tiny\textcircled{2}}
for any
c>0
, we can derive that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}&=&\|Fx^{k+1}-Fy^{k}\|^{2}\leq L^{2}\|x^{k+1}-y^{k}\|^{2}\\
&=&L^{2}\|\eta\hat{w}^{k+1}-\hat{\eta}_{k}\tilde{w}^{k}-\hat{\eta}_{k}e^{k}\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny{\tiny\textcircled{2}}}{\leq}&(1+c)L^{2}\|\eta\hat{w}^{k+1}-\hat{\eta}_{k}\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{(1+c)L^{2}\hat{\eta}_{k}^{2}}{c}\|e^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Multiplying both sides of this inequality by
1+\omega
for some
\omega>0
and rearranging the result, we get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}0&\geq&\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}+\omega\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}-(1+c)(1+\omega)L^{2}\|\eta\hat{w}^{k+1}-\hat{\eta}_{k}\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{(1+c)}{c}(1+\omega)L^{2}\hat{\eta}_{k}^{2}\|e^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

For simplicity of notations, we denote
M_{c}:=(1+c)(1+\omega)L^{2}
.
Using this
M_{c}
and expanding the last expression, we obtain

\begin{array}[]{lcl}0&\geq&\omega\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}+\|w^{k+1}\|^{2}-2\langle w^{k+1}-\tilde{w}^{k},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle+(1-M_{c}\eta^{2})\|\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}2(1-M_{c}\eta\hat{\eta}_{k})\langle\tilde{w}^{k},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle-M_{c}\hat{\eta}_{k}^{2}\|\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2}-\frac{M_{c}\hat{\eta}_{k}^{2}}{c}\|e^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Multiplying this inequality by
\frac{\eta b_{k+1}}{2\tau_{k}}
, adding the result to (
118
), and using
\hat{\eta}_{k}=\eta(1-\tau_{k})
and
b_{k}=b_{k+1}(1-\tau_{k})
from (
12
), we can prove that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{T}_{[2]}&:=&b_{k}\langle w^{k},x^{k}-x^{0}\rangle-b_{k+1}\langle w^{k+1},x^{k+1}-x^{0}\rangle\\
&\geq&\frac{\eta b_{k+1}}{2\tau_{k}}\|w^{k+1}\|^{2}+\frac{\eta b_{k+1}(1-M_{c}\eta^{2})}{2\tau_{k}}\|\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}+\frac{\omega\eta b_{k+1}}{2\tau_{k}}\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{r\eta b_{k+1}(1-\tau_{k})^{2}}{2\tau_{k}}\|w^{k}-\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{\eta b_{k+1}(1-\tau_{k})^{2}(2-M_{c}\eta^{2})}{2\tau_{k}}\|\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{\eta b_{k+1}(1-M_{c}\eta^{2})(1-\tau_{k})}{\tau_{k}}\langle\tilde{w}^{k},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle-\frac{\eta b_{k+1}(1-\tau_{k})^{2}}{\tau_{k}}\langle w^{k},\tilde{w}^{k}\rangle\\
&&-{~}\frac{\eta b_{k+1}(1-\tau_{k})^{2}(c+rM_{c}\eta^{2})}{2rc\tau_{k}}\|e^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Utilizing the following two identities

\begin{array}[]{lcl}2(1-\tau_{k})\langle\tilde{w}^{k},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle&=&(1-\tau_{k})^{2}\|\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2}+\|\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}-\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-(1-\tau_{k})\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2},\\
2\langle w^{k},\tilde{w}^{k}\rangle&=&\|w^{k}\|^{2}+\|\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2}-\|w^{k}-\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2},\end{array}

we can further lower bound
\mathcal{T}_{[2]}
above as

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{T}_{[2]}&\geq&\frac{\eta b_{k+1}}{2\tau_{k}}\|w^{k+1}\|^{2}-\frac{\eta b_{k+1}(1-\tau_{k})^{2}}{2\tau_{k}}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{\omega\eta b_{k+1}}{2\tau_{k}}\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\eta b_{k+1}(1-M_{c}\eta^{2})}{2\tau_{k}}\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-(1-\tau_{k})\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{(1-r)\eta b_{k+1}(1-\tau_{k})^{2}}{2\tau_{k}}\|w^{k}-\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{\eta b_{k+1}(1-\tau_{k})^{2}(c+rM_{c}\eta^{2})}{2rc\tau_{k}}\|e^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Substituting
a_{k}:=\frac{\eta b_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}{\tau_{k}}=\frac{\eta b_{k+1}(1-\tau_{k})^{2}}{\tau_{k}}
and
\mathcal{V}_{k}
from (
11
) into the last estimate, we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{V}_{k}-\mathcal{V}_{k+1}&\geq&\frac{\eta b_{k+1}}{2}\big(\frac{1}{\tau_{k}}-\frac{1-\tau_{k+1}}{\tau_{k+1}}\big)\|w^{k+1}\|^{2}+\frac{\omega\eta b_{k+1}}{2\tau_{k}}\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\eta b_{k+1}(1-M_{c}\eta^{2})}{2\tau_{k}}\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-(1-\tau_{k})\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{(1-r)a_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}-\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2}-\frac{(c+rM_{c}\eta^{2})a_{k}}{2rc}\|e^{k}\|^{2},\end{array}

which proves (
13
) after replacing
e^{k}:=u^{k}-Fx^{k}
.

\square

B.2
The Proof of Theorem
3.1

First, employing
\mathcal{L}_{k}
from (
11
), and substituting
\tau_{k}:=\frac{1}{k+\nu}
and
c_{k}:=\frac{(c+rM_{c}\eta^{2})a_{k}}{rc}=\frac{(c+rM_{c}\eta^{2})\eta b_{k}(k+\nu-1)}{rc}
into (
13
), we can prove that

\hskip-4.30554pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{k}-\mathcal{L}_{k+1}&\geq&\frac{\omega\eta b_{k+1}(k+\nu)}{2}\|w^{k+1}\!-\!\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}+\frac{(1-M_{c}\eta^{2})\eta b_{k+1}(k+\nu)}{2}\|\hat{w}^{k+1}\!-\!(1-\tau_{k})\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{(1-r)a_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}-\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2}-\frac{(c+rM_{c}\eta^{2})\eta b_{k+1}(k+\nu)}{2rc}\|u^{k+1}-Fx^{k+1}\|^{2}.\end{array}\hskip-12.91663pt

Next, combining the last estimate and (
10
), and using
w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}=Fx^{k+1}-Fy^{k}
, we can show that

\hskip-4.30554pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{k}-\mathcal{L}_{k+1}&\geq&\frac{\eta b_{k+1}(k+\nu)}{2}\big[\omega-\frac{\kappa(c+rM_{c}\eta^{2})}{rc}\big]\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}+\frac{(1-r)a_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}-\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\eta b_{k+1}(k+\nu)}{2}\big[1-M_{c}\eta^{2}-\frac{\hat{\kappa}\eta^{2}(c+rM_{c}\eta^{2})}{rc}\big]\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-(1-\tau_{k})\tilde{w}^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}\hskip-12.91663pt

If we choose
\eta
and
r
such that
r\in(0,1]
,
M_{c}\eta^{2}\leq 1
, and

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\omega\geq\frac{\kappa(c+rM_{c}\eta^{2})}{rc}\quad\text{and}\quad M_{c}\eta^{2}+\frac{\hat{\kappa}\eta^{2}(c+rM_{c}\eta^{2})}{rc}\leq 1,\end{array}

(119)

then the last estimate leads to
\mathcal{L}_{k+1}\leq\mathcal{L}_{k}
for all
k\geq 0
.

The first condition of (
119
) holds if
0<\eta\leq\frac{\sqrt{c(r\omega-\kappa)}}{\sqrt{r\kappa M_{c}}}
, where
\kappa\leq r\omega
due to the choice of
\omega
.
The second condition of (
119
) holds if
(M_{c}+\hat{\kappa}\omega)\eta^{2}\leq 1
, which is equivalent to
\eta\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{M_{c}+\hat{\kappa}\omega}}
.
Now, for any
r\in(0,1]
, let us choose
\omega:=\frac{2\kappa}{r}
and
c:=r
.
Then, we have
M_{c}+\hat{\kappa}\omega\geq\frac{r\kappa M_{c}}{c(r\omega-\kappa)}=M_{c}
.
Therefore, if we choose
0<\eta\leq\bar{\eta}:=\frac{\sqrt{r}}{\sqrt{(1+r)(r+2\kappa)L^{2}+2\kappa\hat{\kappa}}}
, then
\eta
satisfies both conditions above.
Clearly, this
\bar{\eta}
is given by (
15
).

Finally, since
b_{k+1}=\frac{b_{k}}{1-\tau_{k}}=\frac{b_{k}(k+\nu)}{k+\nu-1}
, by induction, we obtain
b_{k}=\frac{b_{0}(k+\nu-1)}{\nu-1}
for some
b_{0}>0
.
Moreover, we also have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}a_{k}=\frac{\eta b_{0}(k+\nu-1)^{2}}{\nu-1}\quad\text{and}\quad c_{k}=\frac{\eta b_{0}(c+rM_{c}\eta^{2})(k+\nu-1)^{2}}{rc(\nu-1)}.\end{array}

Using these expressions,
\langle w^{k},x^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle\geq 0
for any
x^{\star}\in\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)
, and Young’s inequality in
{\tiny\textcircled{1}}
, we can lower bound
\mathcal{V}_{k}
from (
11
) as

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{V}_{k}&=&\frac{b_{0}\eta(k+\nu-1)^{2}}{2(\nu-1)}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{b_{0}(k+\nu-1)}{\nu-1}\big[\langle w^{k},x^{\star}-x^{0}\rangle+\langle w^{k},x^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle\big]\\
&&+{~}\frac{b_{0}}{\eta(\nu-1)}\|x^{0}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny{\tiny\textcircled{1}}}{\geq}&\frac{b_{0}\eta(k+\nu-1)^{2}}{4(\nu-1)}\|w^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Since we have chosen
u^{0}:=Fx^{0}
, from (
11
), we have
\mathcal{L}_{0}:=\mathcal{V}_{0}+\frac{c_{0}}{2}\|Fx^{0}-u^{0}\|^{2}=\frac{\eta b_{0}}{4(\nu-1)}\|w^{0}\|^{2}+\frac{b_{0}}{\eta(\nu-1)}\|x^{0}-x^{\star}\|^{2}=\frac{b_{0}}{4\eta(\nu-1)}\big[\eta^{2}\|w^{0}\|^{2}+4\|x^{0}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\big]
.
Putting these bounds together, we can derive that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{b_{0}\eta(k+\nu-1)^{2}}{4(\nu-1)}\|w^{k}\|^{2}&\leq&\mathcal{V}_{k}\leq\mathcal{L}_{k}\leq\mathcal{L}_{0}=\frac{b_{0}}{4\eta(\nu-1)}\big[\eta^{2}\|w^{0}\|^{2}+4\|x^{0}-x^{\star}\|^{2}\big],\end{array}

which is exactly (
16
) after substituting
w^{0}:=Fx^{0}+\xi^{0}
and
w^{k}:=Fx^{k}+\xi^{k}
for
\xi^{0}\in Tx^{0}
and
\xi^{k}\in Tx^{k}
, respectively.

\square

Appendix C
The Proof of Technical Results in Section
4

This appendix provides the full proof of Lemmas and Theorems in Section
4
.

C.1
The Proof of Lemma
2

First, let us denote
e^{k}:=u^{k}-Fx^{k}=z^{k}-w^{k}
.
Then, from the second line of (
GFEG
), we can easily show that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}x^{k+1}-x^{k}&=&-\frac{\tau_{k}}{1-\tau_{k}}(x^{k+1}-x^{0})-\frac{\eta}{1-\tau_{k}}\hat{w}^{k+1}+\frac{\beta_{k}}{1-\tau_{k}}w^{k}+\frac{\beta_{k}}{1-\tau_{k}}e^{k},\\
x^{k+1}-x^{k}&=&-\tau_{k}(x^{k}-x^{0})-\eta\hat{w}^{k+1}+\beta_{k}w^{k}+\beta_{k}e^{k}.\end{array}\right.

(120)

Next, since
\Phi
is
\rho
-co-hypomonotone, we have
\langle w^{k+1},x^{k+1}-x^{k}\rangle-\langle w^{k},x^{k+1}-x^{k}\rangle\geq-\rho\|w^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}
.
Substituting (
120
) into this inequality, we can derive

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\tau_{k}\langle w^{k},x^{k}-x^{0}\rangle&-&\frac{\tau_{k}}{1-\tau_{k}}\langle w^{k+1},x^{k+1}-x^{0}\rangle\geq-\rho\|w^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\eta}{1-\tau_{k}}\langle w^{k+1},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle-\frac{\beta_{k}}{1-\tau_{k}}\langle w^{k+1},w^{k}\rangle-\eta\langle w^{k},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle\\
&&+{~}\beta_{k}\|w^{k}\|^{2}-\frac{\beta_{k}}{1-\tau_{k}}\langle w^{k+1}-(1-\tau_{k})w^{k},e^{k}\rangle.\end{array}

Now, multiplying this inequality by
\frac{b_{k}}{\tau_{k}}
and using
b_{k+1}=\frac{b_{k}}{1-\tau_{k}}
, and then applying Young’s inequality
\langle u,v\rangle\leq\frac{\gamma}{2}\|u\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2\gamma}\|v\|^{2}
for
\gamma>0
to
{\tiny\textcircled{1}}
, we get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{[1]}&:=&b_{k}\langle w^{k},x^{k}-x^{0}\rangle-b_{k+1}\langle w^{k+1},x^{k+1}-x^{0}\rangle\\
&\overset{\tiny{\tiny\textcircled{1}}}{\geq}&\frac{\eta b_{k}}{\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\langle w^{k+1},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle-\frac{b_{k}\beta_{k}}{\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\langle w^{k+1},w^{k}\rangle-\frac{\eta b_{k}}{\tau_{k}}\langle w^{k},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle\\
&&+{~}\frac{b_{k}\beta_{k}}{\tau_{k}}\|w^{k}\|^{2}-\frac{b_{k}\rho}{\tau_{k}}\|w^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}-\frac{b_{k}\beta_{k}}{2\gamma\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\|e^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{\gamma b_{k}\beta_{k}}{2\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\|w^{k+1}-(1-\tau_{k})w^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Then, utilizing the following two elementary identities:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}2\langle w^{k+1},w^{k}\rangle&=&\|w^{k+1}\|^{2}+\|w^{k}\|^{2}-\|w^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2},\\
\|w^{k+1}\!-\!(1-\tau_{k})w^{k}\|^{2}&=&\tau_{k}\|w^{k+1}\|^{2}-\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})\|w^{k}\|^{2}+(1\!-\!\tau_{k})\|w^{k+1}\!-\!w^{k}\|^{2},\end{array}

the last inequality leads to

\hskip-8.61108pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{[1]}&:=&b_{k}\langle w^{k},x^{k}-x^{0}\rangle-b_{k+1}\langle w^{k+1},x^{k+1}-x^{0}\rangle\\
&\geq&\frac{\eta b_{k}}{\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\langle w^{k+1}-w^{k},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle+\frac{\eta b_{k}}{1-\tau_{k}}\langle w^{k},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle\\
&&-{~}\frac{b_{k}\beta_{k}(1+\gamma\tau_{k})}{2\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\|w^{k+1}\|^{2}+\frac{b_{k}\beta_{k}[1-2\tau_{k}+\gamma\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})]}{2\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\|w^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{b_{k}[\beta_{k}(1-\gamma+\gamma\tau_{k})-2\rho(1-\tau_{k})]}{2\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\|w^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}-\frac{b_{k}\beta_{k}}{2\gamma\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\|e^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}\hskip-8.61108pt

(121)

Since
x^{k+1}-y^{k}=\hat{\eta}_{k}w^{k}-\eta\hat{w}^{k+1}+\hat{\eta}_{k}e^{k}
from (
GFEG
), by the Lipschitz continuity of
F
and Young’s inequality in
{\tiny\textcircled{2}}
, for any
r>0
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}&=&\|Fx^{k+1}-Fy^{k}\|^{2}\leq L^{2}\|x^{k+1}-y^{k}\|^{2}\\
&=&L^{2}\|\hat{\eta}_{k}w^{k}-\eta\hat{w}^{k+1}+\hat{\eta}_{k}e^{k}\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny{\tiny\textcircled{2}}}{\leq}&(1+r)L^{2}\|\eta\hat{w}^{k+1}-\hat{\eta}_{k}w^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{(1+r)L^{2}\hat{\eta}_{k}^{2}}{r}\|e^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Since
\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}=\mu\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}+(1-\mu)\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}
for any
\mu\in[0,1]
, partly expanding the last estimate yields

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mu\|w^{k+1}\|^{2}&+&\mu\|\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}-2\mu\langle w^{k+1},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle+(1-\mu)\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\\
&\leq&(1+r)L^{2}\eta^{2}\|\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}+(1+r)L^{2}\hat{\eta}_{k}^{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}2(1+r)L^{2}\eta\hat{\eta}_{k}\langle w^{k},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle+\frac{(1+r)L^{2}\hat{\eta}_{k}^{2}}{r}\|e^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Rearranging this inequality, and multiplying the result by
\frac{\eta b_{k}}{2\mu\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}
, we get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{(1+r)L^{2}\eta b_{k}\hat{\eta}_{k}^{2}}{2r\mu\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\|e^{k}\|^{2}&\geq&\frac{(1-\mu)\eta b_{k}}{2\mu\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}-\frac{(1+r)L^{2}\eta b_{k}\hat{\eta}_{k}^{2}}{2\mu\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\|w^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\eta b_{k}}{2\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\|w^{k+1}\|^{2}+\frac{\eta b_{k}[\mu-(1+r)L^{2}\eta^{2}]}{2\mu\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\|\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{\eta b_{k}[\mu-(1+r)L^{2}\eta\hat{\eta}_{k}]}{\mu\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\langle w^{k},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle\\
&&-{~}\frac{\eta b_{k}}{\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\langle w^{k+1}-w^{k},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle.\end{array}

Adding the last inequality to
\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{[1]}
in (
121
), we can derive that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{[2]}&:=&b_{k}\langle w^{k},x^{k}-x^{0}\rangle-b_{k+1}\langle w^{k+1},x^{k+1}-x^{0}\rangle\\
&&+{~}\frac{b_{k}}{2\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\big[\frac{(1+r)L^{2}\eta\hat{\eta}_{k}^{2}}{r\mu}+\frac{\beta_{k}}{\gamma}\big]\|e^{k}\|^{2}\\
&\geq&\frac{b_{k}[\eta-\beta_{k}(1+\gamma\tau_{k})]}{2\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\|w^{k+1}\|^{2}+\frac{b_{k}[\mu\beta_{k}(1-2\tau_{k}+\gamma\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k}))-(1+r)L^{2}\eta\hat{\eta}_{k}^{2}]}{2\mu\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\|w^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\eta b_{k}[\mu-(1+r)L^{2}\eta^{2}]}{2\mu\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\|\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}-\frac{\eta b_{k}[\mu(1-\tau_{k})-(1+r)L^{2}\eta\hat{\eta}_{k}]}{\mu\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\langle w^{k},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle\\
&&+{~}\frac{(1-\mu)\eta b_{k}}{2\mu\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}+\frac{b_{k}[\beta_{k}(1-\gamma+\gamma\tau_{k})-2\rho(1-\tau_{k})]}{2\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\|w^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Finally, substituting
\hat{\eta}_{k}=\eta(1-\tau_{k})
and
b_{k+1}=\frac{b_{k}}{1-\tau_{k}}
into
\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{[2]}
, then using

\begin{array}[]{lcl}2(1-\tau_{k})\langle w^{k},\hat{w}^{k+1}\rangle=(1-\tau_{k})^{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+\|\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}-\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-(1-\tau_{k})w^{k}\|^{2}\end{array}

and rearranging the result, we eventually arrive at

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{[3]}&:=&\frac{b_{k}[\eta(1-\tau_{k})^{2}-\beta_{k}(1-2\tau_{k}+\gamma\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k}))]}{2\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+b_{k}\langle w^{k},x^{k}-x^{0}\rangle\\
&&+{~}\frac{b_{k}[(1+r)L^{2}\eta^{3}\gamma(1-\tau_{k})^{2}+r\mu\beta_{k}]}{2r\mu\gamma\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}\|e^{k}\|^{2}\\
&\geq&\frac{b_{k+1}[\eta-\beta_{k}(1+\gamma\tau_{k})]}{2\tau_{k}}\|w^{k+1}\|^{2}+b_{k+1}\langle w^{k+1},x^{k+1}-x^{0}\rangle\\
&&+{~}\frac{(1-\mu)\eta b_{k+1}}{2\mu\tau_{k}}\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}+\frac{\eta b_{k+1}[\mu-(1+r)L^{2}\eta^{2}]}{2\mu\tau_{k}}\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-(1-\tau_{k})w^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{b_{k+1}[\beta_{k}(1-\gamma+\gamma\tau_{k})-2\rho(1-\tau_{k})]}{2\tau_{k}}\|w^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Using the notations from (
27
) in
\hat{\mathcal{T}}_{[3]}
and rearranging the result yields (
29
).

\square

C.2
The Proof of Lemma
3

Substituting (
21
) into (
29
) with a notice that
w^{k}-\hat{w}^{k}=Fx^{k}-Fy^{k-1}
, and using (
30
), we can derive

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}_{k}&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:FEG4NI_Lyapunov_func}}{=}&\frac{a_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+b_{k}\langle w^{k},x^{k}-x^{0}\rangle+\frac{c_{k}}{2}\|Fx^{k}-u^{k}\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:FEG4NI_key_est2_cond}}{\geq}&\frac{\hat{a}_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+b_{k}\langle w^{k},x^{k}-x^{0}\rangle+\frac{c_{k}}{2}\|Fx^{k}-u^{k}\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:FEG4NI_key_estimate1}}{\geq}&\frac{a_{k+1}}{2}\|w^{k+1}\|^{2}+b_{k+1}\langle w^{k+1},x^{k+1}-x^{0}\rangle+\frac{\alpha_{k+1}}{2}\|w^{k+1}-\hat{w}^{k+1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\hat{\alpha}_{k+1}}{2}\|w^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{\delta_{k+1}}{2}\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-(1-\tau_{k})w^{k}\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:FEG4NI_key_est2_cond}}{\geq}&\frac{a_{k+1}}{2}\|w^{k+1}\|^{2}+b_{k+1}\langle w^{k+1},x^{k+1}-x^{0}\rangle+\frac{\kappa c_{k+1}}{2}\|Fx^{k+1}-Fy^{k}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\hat{\kappa}c_{k+1}}{2}\|w^{k+1}-w^{k}\|^{2}+\frac{\delta_{k+1}}{2}\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-(1-\tau_{k})w^{k}\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:FEG4NI_u_cond}}{\geq}&\frac{a_{k+1}}{2}\|w^{k+1}\|^{2}+b_{k+1}\langle w^{k+1},x^{k+1}-x^{0}\rangle+\frac{c_{k+1}}{2}\|Fx^{k+1}-u^{k+1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\delta_{k+1}}{2}\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-(1-\tau_{k})w^{k}\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:FEG4NI_Lyapunov_func}}{=}&\mathcal{L}_{k+1}+\frac{\delta_{k+1}}{2}\|\hat{w}^{k+1}-(1-\tau_{k})w^{k}\|^{2},\end{array}

which proves (
31
).

\square

C.3
The Proof of Lemma
4

Let us choose
\tau_{k}:=\frac{1}{k+\nu}
for some
\nu>1
and
\beta_{k}:=\beta(1-\tau_{k})
as in (
32
).
We verify the first condition
a_{k}\geq\hat{a}_{k}
of (
30
), which is equivalent to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\eta\big(\frac{1}{\tau_{k-1}}-\frac{1}{\tau_{k}}+1\big)\geq\beta_{k-1}\big(\frac{1}{\tau_{k-1}}+\gamma\big)-\beta_{k}\big[\frac{1-2\tau_{k}}{\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k})}+\gamma\big].\end{array}

Since
\tau_{k}:=\frac{1}{k+\nu}
and
\beta_{k}:=\beta(1-\tau_{k})
, this condition automatically holds.

We still need
\hat{a}_{k}\geq 0
, which is guaranteed if
\eta\geq\beta\big(1-\frac{\tau_{k}}{1-\tau_{k}}+\gamma\tau_{k}\big)
.
Since this holds for all
k\geq 0
, we impose a stricter condition
\beta\leq\frac{\nu\eta}{\nu+\gamma}
as in (
33
).

Next, the second condition
\alpha_{k}\geq\kappa c_{k}
of (
30
) is equivalent to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\beta\leq\frac{\gamma\eta(k+\nu-1)}{\kappa\mu(k+\nu)}\Big[1-\mu-\frac{\kappa(1+r)L^{2}\eta^{2}}{r}\Big],\end{array}

provided that
\kappa(1+r)L^{2}\eta^{2}<(1-\mu)r
.
Since this condition must hold for all
k\geq 0
, we impose
\beta\leq\frac{\gamma\eta(\nu-1)}{\kappa\mu\nu}\left[1-\mu-\frac{\kappa(1+r)L^{2}\eta^{2}}{r}\right]
as in (
33
).

Finally, the third condition
\hat{\alpha}_{k}\geq\kappa c_{k}
of (
30
) becomes

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\beta\Big[1-\gamma+\gamma\tau_{k-1}-\frac{\hat{\kappa}\tau_{k-1}}{\gamma\tau_{k}(1-\tau_{k-1})}\Big]\geq\frac{\hat{\kappa}(1+r)L^{2}\eta^{3}}{r\mu}\cdot\frac{(1-\tau_{k})\tau_{k-1}}{(1-\tau_{k-1})\tau_{k}}+2\rho.\end{array}

Since it must hold for all
k\geq 0
, we impose

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\beta&\geq&\frac{(\nu-2)\gamma}{(\nu-2)\gamma(1-\gamma)-\nu\hat{\kappa}}\Big[\frac{(\nu-1)\hat{\kappa}(1+r)L^{2}\eta^{3}}{(\nu-2)r\mu}+2\rho\Big],\end{array}

provided that
(\nu-2)\gamma(1-\gamma)>\nu\hat{\kappa}
.
This is the first condition in (
33
), which guarantees the third condition of (
30
) to hold.

Overall, we have shown that
\tau_{k}
and
\beta_{k}
chosen by (
32
) satisfy (
30
), where
\beta
satisfies (
33
).
Therefore, we still obtain (
31
).

\square

C.4
The Proof of Theorem
4.1

First, for the right-hand side of (
33
) to be well-defined and for
\delta_{k+1}\geq 0
in (
31
), we need to impose the following conditions:

\begin{array}[]{ll}L^{2}\eta^{2}\leq\frac{(1-\mu)r}{(1+r)\kappa},\quad L^{2}\eta^{2}\leq\frac{\mu}{1+r},\ \ \text{and}\ \ \nu\hat{\kappa}<(\nu-2)(1-\gamma)\gamma.\end{array}

(122)

Furthermore, for
\nu>2
, we can impose a stricter condition
\beta<\frac{\eta}{1+\gamma}<\frac{\nu\eta}{\nu+\gamma}
to guarantee the third line of (
33
).
Now, we consider the following two cases.

Case 1.
If
\kappa=0
, then the first condition of (
122
) automatically holds.
The second condition of (
122
) becomes
\eta\leq\frac{\sqrt{\mu}}{L\sqrt{1+r}}
.
Therefore, we consider the following two sub-cases:

(i)

If
\hat{\kappa}=0
, then the last condition of (
122
) holds automatically.
Moreover, we can choose

\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{2\rho}{1-\gamma}\leq\beta<\frac{\eta}{1+\gamma}\end{array}

to guarantee (
33
) in Lemma
4
.
Let
\gamma\to 0^{+}
,
r\to 0^{+}
and
\mu=1
, the last two conditions reduce to
2\rho\leq\beta<\eta
.
However, since
\eta\leq\frac{\sqrt{\mu}}{L\sqrt{1+r}}=\frac{1}{L}
, we finally get
2\rho\leq\beta<\eta\leq\frac{1}{L}
, provided that
2L\rho\leq 1
.

(ii)

If
\hat{\kappa}>0
, then the last condition of (
122
) becomes
0<\hat{\kappa}<\frac{(\nu-2)(1-\gamma)\gamma}{\nu}
.
We can choose
\gamma:=\frac{1}{2}
to obtain
0<\hat{\kappa}<\frac{\nu-2}{4\nu}
, provided that
\nu>2
.
In this case, if we choose
\mu=1
and
r=1
, then (
33
) in Lemma
4
holds if

\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{2(\nu-2)}{\nu-2-4\nu\hat{\kappa}}\big[\frac{2(\nu-1)\hat{\kappa}L^{2}\eta^{3}}{\nu-2}+2\rho\big]\leq\beta<\frac{2\eta}{3}<\frac{2\nu\eta}{2\nu+1},\end{array}

leading to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}2\rho<\frac{(\nu-2-4\nu\hat{\kappa})\eta}{3(\nu-2)}-\frac{2(\nu-1)\hat{\kappa}L^{2}\eta^{3}}{\nu-2}=\frac{[(\nu-2-4\nu\hat{\kappa})-6(\nu-1)\hat{\kappa}L^{2}\eta^{2}]\eta}{3(\nu-2)}.\end{array}

If we impose
L^{2}\eta^{2}\leq\frac{\nu-2-4\nu\hat{\kappa}}{12(\nu-1)\hat{\kappa}}
, then to guarantee that
\eta\leq\frac{\sqrt{\mu}}{L\sqrt{1+r}}=\frac{1}{L\sqrt{2}}
, we require
\eta\leq\frac{\sigma_{1}}{L}
, where
\sigma_{1}^{2}:=\min\left\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{\nu-2-4\nu\hat{\kappa}}{12(\nu-1)\hat{\kappa}}\right\}
.
Using this bound, the last inequality holds if
2\rho<\frac{(\nu-2-4\nu\hat{\kappa})\sigma_{1}}{6(\nu-2)L}
, or equivalently,
L\rho<\frac{(\nu-2-4\nu\hat{\kappa})\sigma_{1}}{12(\nu-2)}
.
Moreover, we can choose

\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{2(\nu-2)}{\nu-2-4\nu\hat{\kappa}}\big[\frac{2(\nu-1)\hat{\kappa}\eta\sigma_{1}^{2}}{\nu-2}+2\rho\big]\leq\beta<\frac{2\eta}{3}.\end{array}

Case 2.

If
\kappa>0
, then we can choose
r=1
and
\mu:=\frac{r}{\kappa+r}=\frac{1}{\kappa+1}
such that
L^{2}\eta^{2}\leq\frac{(1-\mu)r}{(1+r)\kappa}=\frac{\mu}{1+r}=\frac{r}{(1+r)(\kappa+r)}
.
Hence, we get
\eta\leq\frac{\sqrt{r}}{L\sqrt{(1+r)(\kappa+r)}}=\frac{1}{L\sqrt{2(\kappa+1)}}
.
We instead impose the following condition
\eta\leq\frac{1}{2L\sqrt{\kappa+1}}
, which is stricter than the last one.
Then, we have
1-\mu-\frac{(1+r)\kappa L^{2}\eta^{2}}{r}\geq\frac{\kappa}{2(\kappa+1)}
.

Now, we consider the following two sub-cases.

(i)

If
\hat{\kappa}=0
, then the last condition of (
122
) automatically holds.
In addition, we can choose
\gamma:=\frac{1}{2}
, and thus (
33
) of Lemma
4
holds if

\begin{array}[]{ll}4\rho\leq\beta\leq\frac{(\nu-1)\eta}{4\nu}<\frac{2\eta}{3}<\frac{2\nu\eta}{2\nu+1}.\end{array}

Moreover, since
\eta\leq\frac{1}{2L\sqrt{\kappa+1}}
, we eventually get
4\rho\leq\beta\leq\frac{(\nu-1)\eta}{4\nu}\leq\frac{1}{8L\nu\sqrt{\kappa+1}}
, provided that
L\rho\leq\frac{\nu-1}{32\nu\sqrt{\kappa+1}}
.

(ii)

If
\hat{\kappa}>0
, then the last condition (
122
) imposes that
0<\hat{\kappa}<\frac{(\nu-2)(1-\gamma)\gamma}{\nu}
.
Again, we can choose
\gamma:=\frac{1}{2}
to obtain
0<\hat{\kappa}<\frac{\nu-2}{4\nu}
, provided that
\nu>2
.
In this case, (
33
) of Lemma
4
holds if

\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{2(\nu-2)}{\nu-2-4\nu\hat{\kappa}}\big[\frac{2(\nu-1)(\kappa+1)\hat{\kappa}L^{2}\eta^{3}}{\nu-2}+2\rho\big]\leq\beta\leq\frac{(\nu-1)\eta}{4\nu}<\frac{2\eta}{3}<\frac{2\nu\eta}{2\nu+1},\end{array}

leading to

\begin{array}[]{ll}2\rho\leq\frac{(\nu-1)(\nu-2-4\nu\hat{\kappa})\eta}{8\nu(\nu-2)}-\frac{2(\nu-1)(\kappa+1)\hat{\kappa}L^{2}\eta^{3}}{\nu-2}=\frac{(\nu-1)\left[\nu-2-4\nu\hat{\kappa}-16\nu(\kappa+1)\hat{\kappa}L^{2}\eta^{2}\right]\eta}{8\nu(\nu-2)}.\end{array}

If we enforce
L^{2}\eta^{2}\leq\frac{\nu-2-4\nu\hat{\kappa}}{32\nu(\kappa+1)\hat{\kappa}}
, then to guarantee
\eta\leq\frac{1}{2L\sqrt{\kappa+1}}
, we require
\eta\leq\frac{\sigma_{2}}{L}
, where
\sigma_{2}^{2}:=\frac{1}{\kappa+1}\min\left\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{\nu-2-4\nu\hat{\kappa}}{32\nu\hat{\kappa}}\right\}
.
Using this bound, the last inequality holds if
2\rho\leq\frac{(\nu-1)(\nu-2-4\nu\hat{\kappa})\sigma_{2}}{16\nu(\nu-2)L}
, or equivalently,
L\rho\leq\frac{(\nu-1)(\nu-2-4\nu\hat{\kappa})\sigma_{2}}{32\nu(\nu-2)}
.
Moreover, we can choose

\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{2(\nu-2)}{\nu-2-4\nu\hat{\kappa}}\big[\frac{2(\nu-1)(\kappa+1)\hat{\kappa}\eta\sigma_{2}^{2}}{\nu-2}+2\rho\big]\leq\beta\leq\frac{(\nu-1)\eta}{4\nu}.\end{array}

Overall, under the choice of parameters as discussed in the two cases above, we can guarantee the conditions in (
31
) of Lemma
4
.
Moreover, in all cases, we also have
\beta<\frac{\eta}{1+\gamma}
, or equivalently,
\eta-(1+\gamma)\beta>0
.

Next, using the update rules (
34
), we have
\beta_{k}=\frac{\beta(k+\nu-1)}{k+\nu}
and
b_{k}=\frac{b_{0}(k+\nu-1)}{\nu-1}
for all
k\geq 0
.
Furthermore, we can easily show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}a_{k}&:=&\frac{b_{k}[\eta-\beta_{k-1}(1+\gamma\tau_{k-1})]}{\tau_{k-1}}\geq\frac{b_{0}[\eta-(1+\gamma)\beta](k+\nu-1)^{2}}{(s-1)}+2\rho b_{k}.\end{array}

Since
x^{\star}\in\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)
, and
w^{k}\in\Phi x^{k}=Fx^{k}+Tx^{k}
, we have
\langle w^{k},x^{k}-x^{\star}\rangle\geq-\rho\|w^{k}\|^{2}
due to the
\rho
-co-hypomonotonicity of
\Phi
. Using this bound and the elementary inequality
uv\leq su^{2}+\frac{1}{4s}v^{2}
for some
s>0
, we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcllcl}\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{k}&:=&\frac{a_{k}}{2}\|w^{k}\|^{2}+b_{k}\langle w^{k},x^{k}-x^{0}\rangle\\
